乙型和丁型肝炎:基于干扰素的治疗——用于临床试验或标准护理——回到未来?

Hepatitis B and D: Interferon‐Based Therapy—For Clinical Trials or Standard of Care—Back to the Future?
作者： Zillah Cargill, Harry L. A. Janssen, Tarik Asselah, Kosh Agarwal
摘要中文翻译摘要 过去三十年来,干扰素 (IFN) 在慢性病毒性乙型肝炎和丁型肝炎的治疗中发挥了重要作用。核苷/核苷酸类似物(NUC)在抑制乙型肝炎病毒方面的广泛应用,其副作用较小,加上最近获得了用于丁型肝炎 治疗 的 bulevirtide 许可,意味着其临床使用进一步下降。聚乙二醇化干扰素α (peg-IFNα) 治疗 效果欠佳,耐受性较差,仅在有限数量的患者中实现了乙型肝炎和丁型肝炎的持久清除。尽管如此,与单独使用 NUC 的患者相比,其在乙型肝炎中的使用显着增加了实现乙型肝炎表面抗原 (HBsAg) 消失的患者数量。有证据表明,peg-IFNα 还与慢性丁型肝炎的长期临床结局的整体改善相关,即使对于许多丁型肝炎病毒载量在停药后反弹的人来说也是如此。最近的临床试验重点关注 peg-IFNα 与新型 抗病毒药物 疗法的联合治疗,以实现乙型肝炎和丁型肝炎的有限治愈。本文重点介绍最新的研究进展,探讨 peg-IFNα 治疗 是否仍可能在乙型肝炎和丁型肝炎 治疗 中发挥重要作用。
查看综述五要素
范围与问题
该综述重点关注基于干扰素的治疗在乙型和丁型肝炎 治疗 中的作用,特别检查其临床用途、有效性和未来应用的潜力。核心问题是聚乙二醇化干扰素 α (peg-IFNα) 治疗 是否仍可能在控制这些感染中发挥重要作用。
证据基础与方法
该综述是对现有文献的叙述性综合,没有明确提及系统搜索方法、数据库或荟萃分析。它包括对该主题的总体概述,并重点介绍了最近的临床试验和研究进展。
共识与结论
该综述的结论是,peg-IFNα 与改善慢性丁型肝炎的长期临床结果相关,即使对于 治疗 后病毒载量反弹的患者也是如此。它还指出,与单独使用核苷/核苷酸类似物 (NUC) 相比,peg-IFNα 已被证明可以增加乙型肝炎表面抗原 (HBsAg) 消失的患者数量。
争议与缺口
该综述强调,peg-IFNα 治疗 由于对乙型肝炎和丁型肝炎的耐受性差且持久清除有限,因此不是最理想的。它还指出,NUC 的使用有所增加,而近期 bulevirtide 用于丁型肝炎的许可进一步减少了 peg-IFNα 的临床使用。
科研与实践启示
该综述建议,未来的研究应侧重于将peg-IFNα与新型抗病毒药物疗法相结合,以实现乙型肝炎和丁型肝炎的有限治愈。这也意味着需要进一步研究peg-IFNα 治疗的长期获益和风险。

Hepatitis B and D: Interferon‐Based Therapy—For Clinical Trials or Standard of Care—Back to the Future?

Authors: Zillah Cargill, Harry L. A. Janssen, Tarik Asselah, Kosh Agarwal
Original abstract (English)
ABSTRACT Interferon (IFN) has historically played a major role in chronic viral hepatitis B and D management over the last three decades. Widening utilisation of nucleoside/nucleotide analogues (NUCs) for hepatitis B viral suppression, which have a better side‐effect profile, alongside the recent licensing of bulevirtide for treatment of hepatitis D, means that its clinical use has further declined. Pegylated‐interferon alpha (peg‐IFNα) treatment is suboptimal with poor tolerability and achieves durable clearance of hepatitis B and D in only a limited number of patients. Nevertheless, its use in hepatitis B has significantly increased the number of patients achieving hepatitis B surface antigen (HBsAg) loss compared to those on NUCs alone. Evidence has shown that peg‐IFNα is also associated with overall improved long‐term clinical outcomes in chronic hepatitis D, even for the many whose hepatitis D viral load rebounds after discontinuation. Clinical trials have recently focused on peg‐IFNα in combination with novel antiviral therapies in the goal for finite cure in hepatitis B and D. This article focuses on recent research advances, examining if peg‐IFNα treatment may still play an important role in hepatitis B and D treatment.
View review five-element analysis
Scope and question
The review focuses on the role of interferon-based therapy in the treatment of hepatitis B and D, specifically examining its clinical use, effectiveness, and potential for future applications. The central question is whether pegylated-interferon alpha (peg-IFNα) treatment may still play an important role in managing these infections.
Evidence base and method
The review is a narrative synthesis of existing literature, with no explicit mention of systematic search methods, databases, or meta-analysis. It includes a general overview of the topic and highlights recent clinical trials and research advances.
Consensus and conclusions
The review concludes that peg-IFNα is associated with improved long-term clinical outcomes in chronic hepatitis D, even for patients whose viral load rebounds after treatment. It also notes that peg-IFNα has been shown to increase the number of patients achieving hepatitis B surface antigen (HBsAg) loss compared to nucleoside/nucleotide analogues (NUCs) alone.
Controversies and gaps
The review highlights that peg-IFNα treatment is suboptimal due to poor tolerability and limited durable clearance of hepatitis B and D. It also points out that the use of NUCs has increased, and the recent licensing of bulevirtide for hepatitis D has further reduced the clinical use of peg-IFNα.
Research and practice implications
The review suggests that future research should focus on combining peg-IFNα with novel antiviral therapies to achieve a finite cure for hepatitis B and D. It also implies that there is a need for further investigation into the long-term benefits and risks of peg-IFNα treatment.

HIV与病毒性肝炎共感染的现状与未来

The present and future of HIV and viral hepatitis co-infection.
作者： Oana Săndulescu, Christoph Boesecke, Sanjay Bhagani, Mojca Matičič, Chien-Ching Hung, Jürgen K Rockstroh, Juan Ambrosioni
摘要中文翻译HIV与肝炎病毒共感染仍然是全球主要的公共卫生挑战,其原因包括共同的传播途径、具有重叠获得方式的人群以及可能加剧的疾病进展。HIV与乙型肝炎病毒(HBV)和丙型肝炎病毒(HCV)的共感染仍然普遍,尤其是在流行区生活的人群、关键人群、注射毒品者、男男性行为者以及来自流行区的移民中。抗逆转录病毒治疗正朝着不含HBV活性的方案转变,这使得某些个体感染或重新激活HBV的风险增加。尽管直接作用抗病毒药物(DAAs)在许多高收入地区显著降低了HCV的活动性感染,但残留的疾病负担、再感染以及对治疗的不平等获取仍然存在。肝炎delta病毒(HDV)继续作为HIV-HBV共感染个体中一种严重但未被充分诊断的共感染存在,且全球关于其诊断的数据仍不充分。急性甲型肝炎和戊型肝炎越来越多地影响HIV感染者,表现为与HIV相关的病例和散发病例,特别是在血清学监测覆盖率不足的背景下。当代管理强调系统性筛查、血清学监测、整合治疗策略以及公共卫生干预措施,以减少HIV感染者与肝炎相关的发病率和死亡率。
查看综述五要素
范围与问题
HIV与肝炎病毒共感染仍然是全球主要的公共卫生挑战,其原因包括共同的传播途径、具有重叠获得方式的人群以及可能加剧的疾病进展。

共识与结论

争议与缺口
尽管直接作用抗病毒药物(DAAs)在许多高收入地区显著降低了HCV的活动性感染,但残留的疾病负担、再感染以及对治疗的不平等获取仍然存在。

The present and future of HIV and viral hepatitis co-infection.

Authors: Oana Săndulescu, Christoph Boesecke, Sanjay Bhagani, Mojca Matičič, Chien-Ching Hung, Jürgen K Rockstroh, Juan Ambrosioni
Original abstract (English)
HIV and hepatitis virus co-infection remains a major global health challenge, driven by shared transmission routes, populations with overlapping modes of acquisition, and potentially compounded disease progression. Co-infections of HIV and hepatitis B virus (HBV) and hepatitis C virus (HCV) remain prevalent, particularly among people who live in endemic areas, key populations, people who inject drugs, men who have sex with men, and migrants from endemic regions. Antiretroviral therapy is shifting towards regimens without HBV activity, increasing the risk for HBV infection or reactivation in some individuals. Although direct-acting antivirals have reduced active HCV infection considerably in many high-income settings, residual disease burden, reinfections, and inequitable access to treatment persist. Hepatitis delta virus continues to represent a severe but underdiagnosed co-infection among individuals with HIV-HBV co-infection, with insufficient global surveillance data. Acute hepatitis A and E increasingly affect people with HIV, presenting both as outbreak-related and sporadic cases, particularly in the context of suboptimal vaccination coverage. Contemporary management emphasises systematic screening, vaccination, integrated antiviral strategies, and public health interventions to reduce liver-related morbidity and mortality in people with HIV.
View review five-element analysis
Scope and question
HIV and hepatitis virus co-infection remains a major global health challenge, driven by shared transmission routes, populations with overlapping modes of acquisition, and potentially compounded disease progression.
Evidence base and method
Co-infections of HIV and hepatitis B virus (HBV) and hepatitis C virus (HCV) remain prevalent, particularly among people who live in endemic areas, key populations, people who inject drugs, men who have sex with men, and migrants from endemic regions.
Consensus and conclusions
Antiretroviral therapy is shifting towards regimens without HBV activity, increasing the risk for HBV infection or reactivation in some individuals.
Controversies and gaps
Although direct-acting antivirals have reduced active HCV infection considerably in many high-income settings, residual disease burden, reinfections, and inequitable access to treatment persist.
Research and practice implications
Hepatitis delta virus continues to represent a severe but underdiagnosed co-infection among individuals with HIV-HBV co-infection, with insufficient global surveillance data.

乙型肝炎病毒和乙型肝炎病毒/丁型肝炎病毒检测:意大利当前状况与展望

Testing for hepatitis C virus and hepatitis B virus/hepatitis D virus: Current scenarios and perspectives in Italy
作者： Francesca Colapietro, Maurizia Rossana Brunetto, Nicola Coppola, Stefano Fagiuoli, Pietro Lampertico
摘要中文翻译
查看综述五要素
范围与问题
Liver disease accounts for approximately 4% of all deaths worldwide, and viral hepatitis affects more than 300 million people.
证据基础与方法
本专家意见审查总结了意大利三级中心积极从事 HCV、HBV 和 HDV 医院检测的经验,并确定了改进检测和与护理联系的实际途径。
共识与结论
在意大利,丙型肝炎病毒 (HCV) 筛查受益于专门的政府资助,而 乙型肝炎病毒 (HBV) 和丁型肝炎病毒 (HDV) 检测则得到科学协会建议的支持,但并未受益于专门的国家公共卫生计划。
争议与缺口
世界卫生组织的目标是通过 疫苗接种 和筛查计划,到 2030 年将新发肝炎感染率减少 90%,死亡人数减少 65%。
科研与实践启示
本文提出了通过以下方式提高意大利病毒性肝炎管理有效性的建议:(1)加强医院单位之间的协调,整合全科医生和医院之间的信息系统;(2)实施集中报告制度和结构化转诊网络,确保检测结果呈阳性的患者及时得到适当的治疗;(3) 提供所需资源,以增加公共成瘾服务 治疗(Servizi per le Dipendenze)和监狱的检测;(4) 促进对高危人群,包括弱势群体和老年人群的筛查;(5) 开展全国病毒性肝炎宣传活动。

Testing for hepatitis C virus and hepatitis B virus/hepatitis D virus: Current scenarios and perspectives in Italy

Authors: Francesca Colapietro, Maurizia Rossana Brunetto, Nicola Coppola, Stefano Fagiuoli, Pietro Lampertico
Original abstract (English)
Liver disease accounts for approximately 4% of all deaths worldwide, and viral hepatitis affects more than 300 million people. The World Health Organization aims to reduce new hepatitis infections by 90% and deaths by 65% by 2030 through vaccination and screening programs. In Italy, screening for hepatitis C virus (HCV) benefits from dedicated government funding, whereas testing for hepatitis B virus (HBV) and hepatitis D virus (HDV) is supported by recommendations from scientific societies but does not benefit from dedicated national public health programmes. This expert-opinion review summarises the experience of tertiary-level Italian centres actively engaged in hospital-based testing for HCV, HBV, and HDV and identifies practical pathways to improve testing and linkage to care. This article presents proposals to improve the effectiveness of viral hepatitis management in Italy by: (1) Strengthening coordination between hospital units and integrating information systems between general practitioners and hospitals; (2) Implementing a centralised reporting system and a structured referral network to ensure that patients with positive test results receive appropriate therapies in a timely manner; (3) Providing the resources needed to increase testing in public addiction treatment services (Servizi per le Dipendenze) and prisons; (4) Promoting screening in high-risk populations, including vulnerable and older populations; and (5) Implementing a national awareness campaign on viral hepatitis. These measures could help Italy make concrete progress towards eliminating HCV infection and controlling HBV/HDV infection within the next decade.

Evidence base and method

Consensus and conclusions

Controversies and gaps
The World Health Organization aims to reduce new hepatitis infections by 90% and deaths by 65% by 2030 through vaccination and screening programs.
Research and practice implications
This article presents proposals to improve the effectiveness of viral hepatitis management in Italy by: (1) Strengthening coordination between hospital units and integrating information systems between general practitioners and hospitals; (2) Implementing a centralised reporting system and a structured referral network to ensure that patients with positive test results receive appropriate therapies in a timely manner; (3) Providing the resources needed to increase testing in public addiction treatment services (Servizi per le Dipendenze) and prisons; (4) Promoting screening in high-risk populations, including vulnerable and older populations; and (5) Implementing a national awareness campaign on viral hepatitis.

慢性乙型肝炎 中代谢功能障碍相关的脂肪肝和 抗病毒药物 反应:系统评价和荟萃分析

Metabolic dysfunction-associated fatty liver and antiviral response in chronic hepatitis B: A systematic review and meta-analysis
作者： Jia Liu, Peng Zhang, Ke-Zhen Hu, Zhi-Hui Zhao, Yong-Ning Xin, Yan-Zhen Bi
摘要中文翻译
查看综述五要素
范围与问题
该综述旨在评估 MAFLD 对 抗病毒药物 治疗 反应和 慢性乙型肝炎 (CHB) 患者长期临床结果的影响。核心问题是 MAFLD 是否影响 CHB 患者对 抗病毒药物 治疗的病毒学、生化和临床反应。
证据基础与方法
本次系统评价和荟萃分析遵循 PRISMA 指南。证据基础包括来自 PubMed、Cochrane 图书馆、EMBASE 和 Web of Science 的截至 2025 年 11 月 30 日发表的研究。基于 抗病毒药物 方案和 MAFLD 诊断方法进行亚组分析,并进行留一敏感性分析以评估研究结果的稳健性。
共识与结论
MAFLD 与较低的病毒学和生化反应率、第 48 周时乙型肝炎表面抗原血清清除的几率较高(特别是在接受核苷(酸)类似物治疗的患者中)以及尽管接受 抗病毒药物 治疗但发生肝细胞癌(HCC)的几率增加有关。然而,在任何时间点均未观察到 MAFLD 与乙型肝炎 e 抗原血清清除之间存在显着关联。
争议与缺口
亚组分析表明,抗病毒药物 方案和 MAFLD 诊断方法部分解释了总体结果的异质性,特别是 乙型肝炎病毒 DNA 清除率和 HCC 方面。证据没有报告 MAFLD 与这些结果相关的具体机制,也没有报告 MAFLD 对其他临床结果(如肝纤维化进展)的影响。
科研与实践启示
研究结果支持将代谢风险评估纳入慢性乙型肝炎的管理,并强调需要对 MAFLD 和代谢功能障碍相关的脂肪性肝病进行标准化定义。

Metabolic dysfunction-associated fatty liver and antiviral response in chronic hepatitis B: A systematic review and meta-analysis

Authors: Jia Liu, Peng Zhang, Ke-Zhen Hu, Zhi-Hui Zhao, Yong-Ning Xin, Yan-Zhen Bi
Original abstract (English)
BACKGROUND Metabolic dysfunction-associated fatty liver disease (MAFLD) frequently coexists with chronic hepatitis B (CHB). However, its association with antiviral treatment responses and long-term clinical outcomes in patients with CHB remains unclear. AIM To systematically evaluate the impact of MAFLD on antiviral treatment responses and long-term clinical outcomes in patients with CHB. METHODS This systematic review and meta-analysis was conducted in accordance with the Preferred Reporting Items for Systematic Reviews and Meta-Analyses guidelines. PubMed, the Cochrane Library, EMBASE, and Web of Science were searched for studies published up to November 30, 2025. Meta-analyses were performed to assess associations between MAFLD and antiviral treatment responses or clinical outcomes. Subgroup analyses were conducted according to antiviral regimen and MAFLD diagnostic approach, and leave-one-out sensitivity analyses were performed to evaluate robustness. RESULTS Thirty-two studies were included, of which 20 evaluated antiviral treatment efficacy and 13 assessed clinical outcomes in patients with CHB and concomitant MAFLD (one study reported both treatment responses and clinical outcomes). MAFLD was associated with a higher rate of hepatitis B surface antigen seroclearance at 48 weeks [odds ratio (OR) = 1.41, 95%CI: 1.28-1.55], although this association was attenuated at 96 weeks (OR = 1.44, 95%CI: 0.96-2.16). No significant association was observed between MAFLD and hepatitis B e antigen seroclearance at any time-point. MAFLD was consistently associated with lower rates of alanine aminotransferase normalization and hepatitis B virus DNA clearance throughout the treatment duration. MAFLD was associated with a significantly increased risk of hepatocellular carcinoma (HCC) despite antiviral therapy (OR = 1.50, 95%CI: 1.01-2.24). Subgroup analyses according to the antiviral regimen revealed distinct patterns, partly explaining the heterogeneity in the overall analyses. Subgroup analyses suggested that the antiviral regimen and MAFLD diagnostic approach partly explained the heterogeneity, particularly for hepatitis B virus DNA clearance and HCC. CONCLUSION MAFLD was associated with lower virological and biochemical response rates, higher odds of hepatitis B surface antigen seroclearance at week 48, particularly among patients receiving nucleos(t)ide analogue therapy, and higher odds of HCC in patients with CHB, but not with hepatitis B e antigen seroclearance. These findings support metabolic risk assessment in CHB management and highlight the need for standardized MAFLD/metabolic dysfunction-associated steatotic liver disease definitions.
View review five-element analysis
Scope and question
The review aims to evaluate the impact of MAFLD on antiviral treatment responses and long-term clinical outcomes in patients with chronic hepatitis B (CHB). The central question is whether MAFLD influences virological, biochemical, and clinical responses to antiviral therapy in CHB.
Evidence base and method
This systematic review and meta-analysis followed the PRISMA guidelines. The evidence base included studies from PubMed, the Cochrane Library, EMBASE, and Web of Science, published up to November 30, 2025. Subgroup analyses were conducted based on antiviral regimen and MAFLD diagnostic approach, and leave-one-out sensitivity analyses were performed to assess the robustness of findings.
Consensus and conclusions
MAFLD was associated with lower virological and biochemical response rates, higher odds of hepatitis B surface antigen seroclearance at week 48, particularly among patients receiving nucleos(t)ide analogue therapy, and increased odds of hepatocellular carcinoma (HCC) despite antiviral therapy. However, no significant association was observed between MAFLD and hepatitis B e antigen seroclearance at any time-point.
Controversies and gaps
Subgroup analyses suggested that antiviral regimen and MAFLD diagnostic approach partly explained the heterogeneity in the overall findings, particularly for hepatitis B virus DNA clearance and HCC. The evidence does not report on the specific mechanisms linking MAFLD to these outcomes or the impact of MAFLD on other clinical outcomes such as liver fibrosis progression.
Research and practice implications
The findings support the integration of metabolic risk assessment in the management of CHB and highlight the need for standardized definitions of MAFLD and metabolic dysfunction-associated steatotic liver disease.

有效乙型肝炎的负面影响因素 疫苗接种

Negatively Impacting Factors Against Effective Hepatitis B Vaccination
作者： Tanzila Rawnuck, Md Selim Reza, Nusrat Jahan, Ali Raza Khan
摘要中文翻译简介:乙型肝炎病毒 感染是全球最严重的公共卫生问题之一。一项研究显示,全球正面临约 3.61% 的乙型肝炎病毒感染负担,不同国家之间存在显着差异。本研究探讨了阻碍有效乙型肝炎 疫苗接种 的负面影响因素。方法:使用 Google Scholar 搜索引擎对已发表的关于阻碍乙型肝炎成功的因素 疫苗接种 的研究进行了回顾。总共研究了 26 篇关于乙型肝炎 疫苗接种 负面影响因素的期刊文章,并确定并分析了过去 10 年中最相关的文章。结果:总体而言,已确定 18 种障碍阻碍乙型肝炎 疫苗 的有效摄取。对乙型肝炎病毒 疫苗接种 缺乏适当的认识和知识被认为是迄今为止最高的因素,占 69% (18),同时,超过 41% (11) 的研究提到父母没有接受足够的教育,其次是 36% (9) 的研究提到对 疫苗接种 的迷信和错误看法或信仰以及文化障碍。另一方面,27% (7) 的评论文章提到了 疫苗 的价格和 疫苗 的不可用。第四个最常提到的障碍是医疗保健提供者缺乏认识,以及医疗保健专业人员对乙型肝炎病毒的了解不足,15% (4) 的审查研究提到了这一点。问题因地区而异。结论:在查明负面影响因素并控制这些因素后,应强调并优先在全国范围内制定适当规划和组织良好的乙型肝炎疫苗接种政策,以实现足够的疫苗覆盖范围,并控制这些因素,以从时代消除乙型肝炎病毒。今日医学 2026 年,第 38 卷 (2):324-328
查看综述五要素
范围与问题
本综述重点关注对乙型肝炎 疫苗接种 有效性产生负面影响的因素。核心问题是确定阻碍乙型肝炎 疫苗 吸收的障碍,并探讨这些因素如何影响 疫苗接种 结果。
证据基础与方法
该审查是使用 Google Scholar 搜索引擎收集已发表的有关阻碍乙型肝炎成功的因素的研究 疫苗接种 进行的。总共研究了 26 篇期刊文章,识别并分析了过去 10 年中最相关的文章。
共识与结论
该审查确定了阻碍乙型肝炎 疫苗 有效吸收的 18 种障碍。缺乏对乙型肝炎的认识和知识 疫苗接种 是最重要的因素,在 69% (18) 的研究中被引用。41% (11) 的研究提到了家长教育不足的问题,而 36% (9) 的研究提到了对疫苗接种 的迷信和错误认知。27% (7) 的评论文章提到了 疫苗 的价格和可用性。审查还强调,问题因地区而异。
争议与缺口
审查指出,有效乙型肝炎 疫苗接种 的障碍在不同地区存在显着差异,表明所面临的挑战缺乏统一性。没有明确提及这些障碍最普遍的特定地区或人群,这代表了证据差距。
科研与实践启示
该审查强调需要制定适当计划和组织良好的乙型肝炎 疫苗接种 政策,以确保足够的 疫苗 覆盖范围。它表明,解决已发现的障碍,例如提高意识和教育,对于根除 乙型肝炎病毒 至关重要。

Negatively Impacting Factors Against Effective Hepatitis B Vaccination

Authors: Tanzila Rawnuck, Md Selim Reza, Nusrat Jahan, Ali Raza Khan
Original abstract (English)
Introduction : Infection by the Hepatitis B virus is one of the greatest global public health issues. One study revealed that the world is facing about 3.61% of the overall HBV infection burden, with significant variation across countries. This study explores the negatively influencing factors that are hampering the effective Hepatitis-B vaccination. Methods: A review of the published studies that were written on the factors that hamper successful hepatitis B vaccination was conducted using the Google Scholar search engine. A total of 26 journal articles on negatively influencing factors of hepatitis B vaccination were studied, and the most relevant items during the last 10 years were identified and analyzed. Results: Overall, 18 barriers have been identified that hinder the effective uptake of hepatitis B vaccines. Those are lack of appropriate awareness and knowledge about Hepatitis B viral vaccination was identified as by far the highest factor and was 69% (18), simultaneiously, not having adequate education among parents is mentioned in more than 41% (11) of studies, followed by superstition and false perception or belief regarding vaccination as well as cultural barriers are also mentioned by 36% (9) of studies. On the other hand, the price of the vaccine and the unavailability of the vaccine are mentioned in 27% (7) of the reviewed articles. The fourth most mentioned barrier was lack of awareness among the health providers, along with inadequate knowledge of HBV among healthcare professionals, which was mentioned by 15% (4) of the reviewed studies. Issues differed from region to region. Conclusion: An appropriately planned and well-organized hepatitis B vaccination policy throughout the nation should be emphasized and prioritized to have adequate vaccine coverage after identifying the negatively influencing factors, and controlling those factors to eradicate the hepatitis B virus from the era. Medicine Today 2026, Vol.38 (2): 324-328
View review five-element analysis
Scope and question
This review focuses on the factors that negatively impact the effectiveness of hepatitis B vaccination. The central question is to identify the barriers that hinder the uptake of hepatitis B vaccines and to explore how these factors influence vaccination outcomes.
Evidence base and method
The review was conducted using the Google Scholar search engine to gather published studies on the factors that hamper successful hepatitis B vaccination. A total of 26 journal articles were studied, with the most relevant items from the last 10 years being identified and analyzed.
Consensus and conclusions
The review identified 18 barriers that hinder the effective uptake of hepatitis B vaccines. Lack of awareness and knowledge about Hepatitis B vaccination was the highest factor, cited in 69% (18) of the studies. Inadequate education among parents was mentioned in 41% (11) of the studies, while superstition and false perceptions about vaccination were noted in 36% (9) of the studies. The price and unavailability of the vaccine were mentioned in 27% (7) of the reviewed articles. The review also highlights that issues differ by region.
Controversies and gaps
The review notes that the barriers to effective hepatitis B vaccination vary significantly across regions, indicating a lack of uniformity in the challenges faced. There is no explicit mention of specific regions or populations where these barriers are most prevalent, which represents an evidence gap.
Research and practice implications
The review emphasizes the need for an appropriately planned and well-organized hepatitis B vaccination policy to ensure adequate vaccine coverage. It suggests that addressing the identified barriers, such as improving awareness and education, is crucial for eradicating the hepatitis B virus.

🚨 突发动态与新闻 / Health News

Published: 2026-11-02  |  Publisher: EurekAlert!
B 常规重要公共卫生新闻

向 功能性治愈 for 慢性乙型肝炎 迈进:中国不断演变的角色 - EurekAlert!

Advancing toward a functional cure for chronic hepatitis B: China's evolving role - EurekAlert!
新闻简报本新闻报道重点介绍了中国在制定 慢性乙型肝炎 (CHB) 战略方面取得的进展。该文章于 2026 年 11 月 2 日由 EurekAlert! 发表,讨论了上海交通大学医学院曹竹君博士和谢庆教授领导的研究。该研究于 2026 年 10 月 26 日发表在《门脉高压和肝硬化》杂志上,研究了临床试验、荟萃分析和国家流行病学调查,以确定可能受益于新兴疗法的患者。中国主导的研究(包括珠穆朗玛峰项目和 ANCHOR 试验)报告称,接受治疗的参与者分别有 20% 和 19% 在第 72 周达到了 功能性治愈,而安慰剂组则没有。然而,大约 80% 的接受治疗的参与者没有达到 功能性治愈,并且这些反应的持久性仍不确定。该研究强调需要根据 HBsAg 水平、病毒活性、免疫状态和肝损伤采取个性化 治疗 方法。这些试验排除了肝硬化患者,限制了研究结果的普遍性。需要进一步的研究来确定个体患者的最佳 治疗 强度并确保安全监测和随访。该文章强调了了解谁从这些疗法中受益最多以及它们如何减少肝硬化、门静脉高压和肝癌等严重并发症的重要性。
查看新闻五要素
时间
The news was published on 2026-11-02. The event date is not explicitly stated in the evidence, but the research discussed was conducted and published as of October 26, 2026.
地点与对象
The research is centered in China, specifically involving the Shanghai Jiao Tong University School of Medicine and Ruijin Hospital. It addresses the population of approximately 75 million people living with chronic HBV infection in China.
事件
本文讨论了中国在制定 慢性乙型肝炎 (CHB) 战略中不断变化的作用,包括临床试验检查、荟萃分析和国家流行病学调查。它强调了在识别可能受益于新兴疗法的患者方面取得的进展以及实现持久 功能性治愈 的挑战。
规模、影响与风险
中国主导的研究,如珠穆朗玛峰项目和 ANCHOR 试验,报告了令人鼓舞的结果,分别有 20% 和 19% 的治疗参与者在第 72 周达到了 功能性治愈。然而,大约 80% 的接受治疗的参与者没有达到 功能性治愈,并且这些反应的持久性仍不确定。该研究还强调了根据 HBsAg 水平、病毒活性、免疫状态和肝损伤情况为个体患者量身定制 治疗 的重要性。
应对、状态与不确定性
该研究正在进行中,建议进行临床登记和长期随访,以评估新兴疗法的有效性,并确定 治疗 反应在治疗停止后是否持续存在。这些试验排除了肝硬化患者,限制了研究结果的适用性。需要进一步的研究来确定每位患者正确的 治疗 强度,并确保适当的安全监测和随访。
B routine priorityPublic-health news

Advancing toward a functional cure for chronic hepatitis B: China's evolving role - EurekAlert!

News Brief
This news report highlights China's advancements in the development of treatment strategies for chronic hepatitis B (CHB). Published on November 2, 2026, by EurekAlert!, the article discusses research led by Dr. Zhujun Cao and Professor Qing Xie from Shanghai Jiao Tong University School of Medicine. The study, published in the Portal Hypertension & Cirrhosis journal on October 26, 2026, examines clinical trials, meta-analyses, and national epidemiological surveys to identify patients who may benefit from emerging therapies. China-led studies, including the Everest Project and ANCHOR trial, reported that 20% and 19% of treated participants achieved a functional cure at week 72, respectively, compared to none in the placebo groups. However, approximately 80% of treated participants did not achieve a functional cure, and the durability of these responses remains uncertain. The research emphasizes the need for personalized treatment approaches based on HBsAg levels, viral activity, immune status, and liver damage. The trials excluded patients with cirrhosis, limiting the generalizability of the findings. Further research is required to determine the optimal treatment intensity for individual patients and to ensure safety monitoring and follow-up. The article underscores the importance of understanding who benefits most from these therapies and how they can reduce serious complications like cirrhosis, portal hypertension, and liver cancer.
Original source text (English)
Advanced Search Home News Releases Multimedia Meetings Login Register News Release 2-Nov-2026 Advancing toward a functional cure for chronic hepatitis B: China's evolving role New treatment strategies could help identify patients most likely to benefit from shorter courses of therapy Reports and Proceedings Chinese Medical Journals Publishing House Co., Ltd. Facebook X LinkedIn WeChat Bluesky Message WhatsApp Email image: Chronic hepatitis B remains a major global health challenge, driving the search for more effective treatments. view more Credit: Dr. Zhujun Cao and Professor Qing Xie from Shanghai Jiao Tong University School of Medicine, China Image source link: https://onlinelibrary.wiley.com/doi/10.1002/poh2.70073?prg140729=e718ca3f-0104-4d08-b462-4fbdbf86e9fa Chronic hepatitis B (CHB) remains a global health challenge and a leading cause of cirrhosis, portal hypertension, and liver cancer. Although vaccination and antiviral medicines have improved prevention and management, a lasting cure remains difficult. Current treatments suppress hepatitis B virus (HBV) replication, but many patients need long-term therapy. Developing treatments that maintain viral control after medication stops remains a research priority. China has made progress in preventing HBV infections through infant vaccination and measures to prevent mother-to-child transmission. However, approximately 75 million people in the country still live with chronic HBV infection. Against this backdrop, Dr. Zhujun Cao, Professor Qing Xie, and Dr. Dandan Weng from the Department of Infectious Diseases at Ruijin Hospital, Shanghai Jiao Tong University School of Medicine, China, examined China's contribution to developing treatments for CHB. Their perspective has been published online in Portal Hypertension & Cirrhosis journal on October 26, 2026. The authors examined published clinical trials, meta-analyses, national epidemiological surveys, and cohort studies. Their perspective considered established medicines, emerging treatments, safety, patient selection, and long-term outcomes in the pursuit of a functional cure. Existing antiviral medicines, including entecavir and tenofovir, can suppress HBV replication. However, hepatitis B surface antigen (HBsAg), a protein produced during infection, becomes undetectable in only around 2–5% of patients receiving long-term treatment. Pegylated interferon-α (PegIFNα), which stimulates the immune response against HBV, may help selected patients, particularly those with lower HBsAg levels before treatment. China-led studies, including the Everest Project and ANCHOR trial, reported encouraging results, although side effects and monitoring can limit wider use. One important development is bepirovirsen, which targets HBV RNA and reduces viral protein production. In the Phase III B-Well 1 and B-Well 2 trials, functional cure at week 72 was achieved by 20% and 19% of treated participants, respectively, compared with none in the placebo groups. Responses were more common among participants with lower HBsAg levels before treatment. However, approximately four in five treated participants did not achieve functional cure, and durability beyond reported follow-up remains uncertain. The trials excluded patients with cirrhosis, limiting how broadly the findings can be applied. “Although some emerging treatments can reduce viral markers, these changes do not necessarily lead to a lasting functional cure. Further research is needed to determine which approaches can maintain viral control after treatment stops,” shares Prof. Xie. Other treatments developed in China or evaluated with Chinese research participation target viral RNA, interfere with formation of the virus's protective protein shell, or stimulate immune responses. Some candidates have shown promising biological effects, while others have produced limited benefits or failed to meet their clinical objectives. Changes in viral markers do not always translate into lasting patient benefits. The authors highlight the need to tailor treatment to individual patients. HBsAg levels, viral activity, immune status, and liver damage may help identify who is most likely to benefit. Clinical registries and long-term follow-up could assess effectiveness, determine whether responses persist after treatment stops, and identify patients needing further care. Research involving underrepresented groups and populations with different HBV genotypes is also important. “The goal is not simply to provide more treatment, but to identify the right treatment intensity for each patient. Understanding who is most likely to benefit, while ensuring appropriate safety monitoring and follow-up, will be essential to developing more personalized approaches to CHB,” notes Dr. Cao. Overall, the perspective highlights progress in developing treatments for CHB while emphasizing that a safe, lasting functional cure remains out of reach for many patients. Further research is needed to determine which patients benefit most from emerging therapies, how long treatment responses persist, and whether these approaches can reduce serious complications such as cirrhosis, portal hypertension, and liver cancer. *** Reference DOI: 10.1002/poh2.70073 About Shanghai Jiao Tong University Shanghai Jiao Tong University (SJTU) is a leading public research university in Shanghai, China, with a history dating back to 1896. It is recognized for its academic excellence, research innovation, and contributions to science, engineering, medicine, and technology. The university offers a broad range of undergraduate and postgraduate programs and maintains collaborations with universities and research institutions worldwide. Its affiliated hospitals and medical schools contribute significantly to clinical research, biomedical sciences, and healthcare innovation. Through interdisciplinary research, advanced facilities, and international partnerships, SJTU plays an important role in China's higher education and global scientific research community. Website: https://en.sjtu.edu.cn/ About Professor Qing Xie from Shanghai Jiao Tong University School of Medicine, China Prof. Qing Xie is the Director of the Department of Infectious Diseases at Ruijin Hospital and Director of the Severe Viral Hepatitis Clinical Center at Shanghai Jiao Tong University School of Medicine, China. Her research focuses on the clinical management of hepatitis B and C, host immune responses to viral infections, liver injury, and liver fibrosis. She has led multicenter clinical trials involving viral hepatitis and established long-term clinical databases and sample banks for patients with hepatitis B and C. She has published over 100 research articles. About Dr. Zhujun Cao from Shanghai Jiao Tong University School of Medicine, China Dr. Zhujun Cao is a physician and researcher affiliated with the Department of Infectious Diseases at Ruijin Hospital, Shanghai Jiao Tong University School of Medicine, China. His research focuses on hepatology and infectious diseases, particularly the mechanisms underlying organ failure in cirrhosis and the development of novel therapeutic approaches. He is involved in research on the immunopathogenesis of acute-on-chronic liver failure, using clinical research and multi-omics approaches to investigate disease mechanisms and identify potential therapeutic targets. Funding information This work was supported in part by the National Science and Technology Major Project of China (2025ZD01906200, Prevention and Control of Emerging and Major Infectious Diseases) and the National Ten Thousand Talents Program for Young Top-notch Talents. View five news elements
Time
The news was published on 2026-11-02. The event date is not explicitly stated in the evidence, but the research discussed was conducted and published as of October 26, 2026.
Location and population
The research is centered in China, specifically involving the Shanghai Jiao Tong University School of Medicine and Ruijin Hospital. It addresses the population of approximately 75 million people living with chronic HBV infection in China.
Event
The article discusses China's evolving role in the development of treatment strategies for chronic hepatitis B (CHB), including the examination of clinical trials, meta-analyses, and national epidemiological surveys. It highlights the progress made in identifying patients who may benefit from emerging therapies and the challenges in achieving a lasting functional cure.
Scale, impact and risk
China-led studies, such as the Everest Project and ANCHOR trial, reported encouraging results with 20% and 19% of treated participants achieving a functional cure at week 72, respectively. However, approximately 80% of treated participants did not achieve a functional cure, and the durability of these responses remains uncertain. The research also emphasizes the importance of tailoring treatment to individual patients based on HBsAg levels, viral activity, immune status, and liver damage.
Response, status and uncertainty
The research is ongoing, with clinical registries and long-term follow-up being proposed to assess the effectiveness of emerging therapies and determine whether treatment responses persist after therapy stops. The trials excluded patients with cirrhosis, limiting the applicability of the findings. Further research is needed to identify the right treatment intensity for each patient and to ensure appropriate safety monitoring and follow-up.

突破: FDA 批准首个为 慢性乙型肝炎 提供“功能性治愈”的药物 - ScienceAlert

Breakthrough: FDA Approves First Drug Offering a 'Functional Cure' For Chronic Hepatitis B - ScienceAlert
新闻简报根据《新英格兰医学杂志》(N5、N26)发表的 3 期临床试验结果,美国食品和药物管理局 (FDA) 已批准贝匹罗韦森(商品名 Hibsago)作为第一个为 慢性乙型肝炎 (CHB) 提供“功能性治愈”的药物。该药物将血液中的病毒抑制到不可检测的水平,并且在有反应的患者中,即使在 治疗 停止后也能保持病毒的控制(N9,N10)。它通过每周注射给药,可以与现有的 治疗 一起使用,允许一些患者停止服药 (N11)。然而,该疗法的长期有效性尚未得到证实,因为 治疗 (N16) 后病毒的痕迹可能会持续超过 24 周。FDA 指出,一些患者出现了严重的副作用,包括肝脏炎症,需要定期监测(N15)。此次批准标志着慢性乙型肝炎的治疗取得了重大进展,慢性乙型肝炎是一种影响全球数百万人的疾病,每年导致超过一百万人死亡(N4、N25)。
查看新闻五要素
时间
The FDA approved the drug bepirovirsen for chronic hepatitis B treatment on 2026-11-05 (N4). The phase 3 trial results were published in the New England Journal of Medicine (N26). The trial showed that the drug was effective in 233 of 1,220 patients (N13).
地点与对象
该批准与美国相关,慢性乙型肝炎 是美国的一项重大公共卫生挑战,影响数百万人 (N25)。
事件
根据 3 期临床试验结果 (N5),FDA 批准贝匹罗韦森 (Hibsago) 作为第一个为 慢性乙型肝炎 提供“功能性治愈”的药物。
规模、影响与风险
The drug achieved a functional cure response in 19 percent of participants (233 out of 1,220) in phase 3 trials (N9). In those patients, the virus remained undetectable for at least 24 weeks after treatment ended (N10). However, it is not yet proven if the cure lasts for years (N16).
应对、状态与不确定性
FDA已批准该药物,3期试验结果已发表在《新英格兰医学杂志》(N26)上。该药物通过每周注射给药,并且可以与现有的 治疗 (N11) 组合。一些患者出现了严重的副作用,包括肝脏炎症,需要进行监测(N15)。
B routine priorityPublic-health news

Breakthrough: FDA Approves First Drug Offering a 'Functional Cure' For Chronic Hepatitis B - ScienceAlert

News Brief
The US Food and Drug Administration (FDA) has approved bepirovirsen, marketed as Hibsago, as the first drug offering a 'functional cure' for chronic hepatitis B (CHB), based on phase 3 clinical trial results published in the New England Journal of Medicine (N5, N26). The drug suppresses the virus to undetectable levels in the blood and, in patients who respond, keeps it under control even after treatment stops (N9, N10). It is administered via weekly injections and can be used alongside existing treatments, allowing some patients to discontinue pills (N11). However, the cure's long-term effectiveness remains unproven, as traces of the virus may persist beyond 24 weeks post-treatment (N16). The FDA notes that some patients experienced serious side effects, including liver inflammation, which requires regular monitoring (N15). This approval marks a significant advancement in managing CHB, a disease affecting millions globally and responsible for over a million deaths annually (N4, N25).
Original source text (English)

Daily roundup Spark: Our top stories Does Pineapple belong on a burger? Breakthrough: FDA Approves First Drug Offering a 'Functional Cure' For Chronic Hepatitis B Health05 November 2026 By David Nield (THOM LEACH/Science Photo Library RF/Getty Images) There's new hope for the millions of people affected by chronic hepatitis B (CHB), a liver condition that can lead to cancer, scarring, or organ failure, and which is responsible for over a million deaths worldwide each year. Following the results of two phase 3 clinical trials, published in the New England Journal of Medicine, the US Food and Drug Administration (FDA) has approved the drug bepirovirsen for CHB treatment, under the brand name Hibsago. GSK, the pharmaceutical company behind the drug's development, describes bepirovirsen as the first "functional cure" for CHB, meaning it doesn't just manage the virus causing the infection – as existing treatments do – but suppresses it to undetectable levels in the blood. What's more, in patients who respond, the infection stays under control even after bepirovirsen treatment is stopped, which is again in contrast to the drugs currently prescribed for CHB. "This is the start of a new era in treating people living with CHB," says gastroenterologist Norah Terrault, from the Keck Medical Center at the University of Southern California. "Bepirovirsen offers patients the potential to achieve functional cure after a short period of treatment, a long-standing goal for patients and physicians alike." frameborder="0′′ allow="accelerometer; autoplay; clipboard-write; encrypted-media; gyroscope; picture-in-picture; web-share" referrerpolicy="strict-origin-when-cross-origin" allowfullscreen> The phase 3 trial results showed that a six-month course of bepirovirsen achieved a functional cure response in 233 of 1,220 participants (19 percent), compared to 0 out of 614 participants on a placebo drug. In those patients, the virus stayed undetectable for at least 24 weeks after all treatment stopped (72 weeks after the trial began). The drug is administered via weekly injections and was given alongside existing pill treatments, which could then also be stopped for patients given the all-clear. "For the first time, with Hibsago, US patients have the potential to achieve functional cure after a six-month treatment course, with the added convenience of the option of self-administration after the first dose," says Tony Wood, Chief Scientific Officer at GSK, the pharmaceutical company developing the drug. Across two trials, bepirovirsen was effective in 233 of 1,220 patients. (Hou et al., N. Engl. J. Med., 2026) It's not all completely positive. Some patients did experience serious side effects, including liver inflammation, which the FDA says should be monitored for via regular blood tests. Traces of the virus do stick around, too – it's yet to be proven if the cure holds for years after treatment has ended. However, the improvements from current treatments are stark enough that the researchers are confident that a significant number of patients are going to benefit, and that's rubber-stamped by the FDA approval. "For many people living with chronic hepatitis B, treatment can mean taking antiviral medication for years to keep the virus suppressed," says Wendy Carter, the Acting Director at the FDA's Office of Infectious Diseases. "Hibsago provides a new treatment approach that gives some patients the possibility of maintaining control of the virus after all chronic HBV treatment has ended. This approval represents an important advance for people living with this serious disease." Some CHB patients responded better to bepirovirsen than others. Lower levels of a key CHB protein at the start of treatment – indicating a less active infection – meant a higher chance of the new drug working. That's something that could be tested for before bepirovirsen is prescribed. The work doesn't stop here. The researchers want to see how the new treatment might be combined with other drugs to become even more effective, and GSK wants to make it available to more patients worldwide. "CHB is a major public health challenge, affecting millions of people in the US and accounting for approximately 56 percent of all global liver cancer cases," says Chari Cohen, the President of the Hepatitis B Foundation. "Today marks significant progress towards our aim of transforming the way CHB is managed with improvements in screening, diagnosis, and the availability of innovative treatment options for our community." The results of the phase 3 clinical trials have been published in the New England Journal of Medicine. This article was fact-checked by Rebecca Dyer and edited by Rebecca Dyer. FDA 批准 Hibsago 用于治疗慢性 HBV 感染 - 药房实践新闻

FDA Approves Hibsago for Chronic HBV Infection - Pharmacy Practice News
新闻简报FDA 已批准贝匹罗韦森 (Hibsago) 用于治疗 慢性乙型肝炎 病毒 (HBV) 感染的 治疗 成人无肝硬化患者,这些患者正在接受核苷/核苷酸类似物逆转录酶抑制剂治疗,并已抑制 HBV DNA,且 HBsAg 水平等于或低于 3,000 IU/mL。此次批准是在第 3 期试验的积极数据之后进行的,该数据显示,接受 Hibsago 治疗的患者的 功能性治愈 率为 19%,而仅接受标准护理的患者则没有。治疗 组的大多数患者报告了不良事件,但 Hibsago 组和标准护理组中只有 7% 和 4% 经历了严重不良事件。该批准是根据 FDA 优先审查和突破性治疗指定授予的,标志着 HBV 治疗 的重大进步。该药物现已可供符合条件的患者使用,在短期 治疗 后提供潜在的 功能性治愈。
查看新闻五要素
时间
FDA 于 2026 年 11 月 2 日批准了 Hibsago。该批准是根据 FDA 优先审查和突破性疗法指定授予的。
地点与对象
该批准适用于正在接受核苷/核苷酸类似物逆转录酶抑制剂治疗且已抑制 HBV DNA 且 HBsAg 水平等于或低于 3,000 IU/mL 的无肝硬化成年患者。
事件
FDA 批准贝匹罗韦森(Hibsago)用于治疗 慢性乙型肝炎 病毒(HBV)感染的 治疗。该批准基于积极的 3 期试验数据。
规模、影响与风险
在 B-WELL 3 期试验中,在服用贝匹罗韦森的 HBsAg 水平等于或低于 3,000 IU/mL 的成人中观察到 功能性治愈 率为 19%,而仅接受标准护理的成人则没有 功能性治愈 率。双臂的大多数患者均报告了不良事件,但贝匹罗韦森组和标准治疗组中分别只有 7% 和 4% 的患者经历了严重不良事件。
应对、状态与不确定性
The drug is now available for eligible adults without cirrhosis. The findings are based on phase 3 trial data and are not subject to further investigation.
B routine priorityPublic-health news

FDA Approves Hibsago for Chronic HBV Infection - Pharmacy Practice News

News Brief
The FDA has approved bepirovirsen (Hibsago) for the treatment of chronic hepatitis B virus (HBV) infection in adult patients without cirrhosis who are receiving nucleoside/nucleotide analog reverse transcriptase inhibitor therapy and have suppressed HBV DNA with HBsAg levels at or less than 3,000 IU/mL. This approval follows positive phase 3 trial data, which showed a 19% functional cure rate in patients treated with Hibsago, compared to none on the standard of care alone. Adverse events were reported by a majority of patients in both treatment groups, but only 7% in the Hibsago group and 4% in the standard-of-care group experienced severe adverse events. The approval was granted under FDA priority review and Breakthrough Therapy designation, marking a significant advancement in HBV treatment. The drug is now available for eligible patients, offering a potential functional cure after a short period of treatment.
Original source text (English)

Send Close FDA News Share on Facebook Share on LinkedIn Share on X Email Article Copy Article Url Print NOVEMBER 2, 2026 FDA Approves Hibsago for Chronic HBV Infection Originally published by our sister publication Infectious Disease Special Edition By Meaghan Lee Callaghan The FDA has approved bepirovirsen (Hibsago, GSK) for the treatment of chronic hepatitis B virus (HBV) infection. The drug, a first-in-class antisense oligonucleotide, is approved for use in adult patients without cirrhosis who are receiving nucleoside/nucleotide analog reverse transcriptase inhibitor therapy, who have suppressed HBV DNA, and with HBV surface antigen (HBsAg) levels at or less than 3,000 IU/mL, as determined by an FDA-authorized test. The approval, which was granted under FDA priority review and Breakthrough Therapy designation, follows positive phase 3 data. In the B-WELL phase 3 trials, the researchers found functional cure rates of 19% in adults with HBsAg levels at or less than 3,000 IU/mL taking bepirovirsen, compared with none of those on the current standard of care alone (N Engl J Med 2026;394[24]:2395-2406). Although a majority of patients in both arms reported experiencing adverse events, only 7% of patients in the bepirovirsen and 4% in the standard-of-care arms reported severe adverse events. “This is the start of a new era in treating people living with [chronic HBV],” study investigator Norah Terrault, MD, MPH, the chief of gastroenterology and hepatology at Keck Medicine of USC, in Los Angeles, said in a press release. “Bepirovirsen offers patients the potential to achieve functional cure after a short period of treatment, a long-standing goal for patients and physicians alike." B 常规重要公共卫生新闻

问答:优先考虑乙型肝炎 疫苗接种,筛查糖尿病患者

Q&A: Prioritize hepatitis B vaccination, screening for people living with diabetes
新闻简报This article, published on November 05, 2026, discusses the increased risk of hepatitis B virus (HBV) infection in people living with diabetes. It highlights that adults with type 1 or type 2 diabetes have a higher risk for HBV, with acute infection rates about twice that of people without diabetes. The American Diabetes Association recommends HBV vaccination for all adults with diabetes under 60 years old, while those aged 60 or older should be considered for vaccination based on individual risk factors. The CDC advises baseline testing for HBV markers for patients starting hemodialysis, with vaccination recommended as early as possible. For those vaccinated after dialysis initiation, higher-dose regimens are advised, followed by anti-HBs testing. Ongoing HBV surveillance should be guided by immune status, and patients should also be screened for HCV at dialysis initiation and every 6 months thereafter. The article emphasizes the importance of coordinated care for managing HBV and other related conditions in diabetes patients.
查看新闻五要素
时间
The article was published on November 05, 2026, and discusses ongoing recommendations for hepatitis B vaccination and screening.
地点与对象
重点是糖尿病患者,特别是患有 1 型或 2 型糖尿病的成年人,以及长期护理机构或透析室的患者。
事件
该文章强调了糖尿病患者感染 乙型肝炎病毒 (HBV) 的风险增加,并强调了 疫苗接种 以及预防和早期检测筛查的重要性。
规模、影响与风险
糖尿病患者感染乙型肝炎病毒的风险较高,其急性感染率约为非糖尿病患者的两倍。慢性 HBV 感染也更有可能是由于 免疫应答 受损所致。由于频繁的血液暴露和共享的 治疗 环境,透析患者的风险增加。
应对、状态与不确定性
CDC 建议对所有开始血液透析的患者进行 HBV 标志物基线检测。建议易感患者使用 疫苗接种,并在透析开始后对接种疫苗的患者进行更高剂量的治疗方案和后续检测。持续的 HBV 监测 以免疫状态为指导,还建议在透析开始时以及此后每 6 个月进行一次 HCV 筛查。
B routine priorityPublic-health news

Q&A: Prioritize hepatitis B vaccination, screening for people living with diabetes

News Brief
This article, published on November 05, 2026, discusses the increased risk of hepatitis B virus (HBV) infection in people living with diabetes. It highlights that adults with type 1 or type 2 diabetes have a higher risk for HBV, with acute infection rates about twice that of people without diabetes. The American Diabetes Association recommends HBV vaccination for all adults with diabetes under 60 years old, while those aged 60 or older should be considered for vaccination based on individual risk factors. The CDC advises baseline testing for HBV markers for patients starting hemodialysis, with vaccination recommended as early as possible. For those vaccinated after dialysis initiation, higher-dose regimens are advised, followed by anti-HBs testing. Ongoing HBV surveillance should be guided by immune status, and patients should also be screened for HCV at dialysis initiation and every 6 months thereafter. The article emphasizes the importance of coordinated care for managing HBV and other related conditions in diabetes patients.
Original source text (English)
Diabetes In Real Life By Susan Weiner, MS, RDN, CDN, CDCES, FADCES By Arpan Mohanty, MD, MSc Fact checked by Richard Smith Purpose-built for physicians Learn more Read more Q&A: Prioritize hepatitis B vaccination, screening for people living with diabetes November 05, 2026 6 min read By Susan Weiner, MS, RDN, CDN, CDCES, FADCES By Arpan Mohanty, MD, MSc Fact checked by Richard Smith Add topic to email alerts Receive an email when new articles are posted on Please provide your email address to receive an email when new articles are posted on . Added to email alerts You've successfully added to your alerts. You will receive an email when new content is published. Click Here to Manage Email Alerts You've successfully added to your alerts. You will receive an email when new content is published. Click Here to Manage Email Alerts We were unable to process your request. Please try again later. If you continue to have this issue please contact customerservice@slackinc.com . Key takeaways: People living with diabetes have increased risk for hepatitis B virus. Vaccination and screening are key to HBV prevention and early detection. Susan Weiner In this Diabetes in Real Life column, Susan Weiner, MS, RDN, CDN, CDCES, FADCES, talks with Arpan Mohanty, M D , MSc, about why hepatitis B warrants greater attention in diabetes care and what to know about vaccination, screening and more. Weiner: What is the clinical guidance for hepatitis vaccination for people living with diabetes? Mohanty: The American Diabetes Association recommends hepatitis B virus vaccination for all adults with diabetes younger than 60 years. For those aged 60 years or older, vaccination should be considered based on individual risk factors, such as exposure risk, living situation and other health conditions, consistent with Advisory Committee on Immunization Practices guidance. Weiner: What populations are at increased risk? Mohanty: Adults with type 1 or type 2 diabetes have a higher risk for HBV . Among adults aged 19 to 59 years, the risk for acute infection is about twice that of people without diabetes. Diabetes may impair immune responses, reducing the likelihood of HBV clearance and increasing the risk for chronic infection. Risk may be especially high in long-term care settings or when blood glucose monitoring equipment is shared or not properly disinfected. In addition, vaccination is particularly important for people with diabetes, as they are more likely to develop metabolic dysfunction-associated steatotic liver disease (MASLD), which can lead to metabolic dysfunction-associated steatohepatitis (MASH), cirrhosis or liver cancer. Preventing HBV with vaccination helps protect a liver that may already be vulnerable. Weiner: Describe the overlap of chronic kidney disease , diabetes, hypertension, inflammation and coordinated cardiometabolic management for people living with diabetes and hepatitis. Mohanty: In patients with diabetes, hepatitis or liver inflammation most commonly results from viral infection, alcohol-associated liver disease or MASH, the inflammatory form of MASLD. MASLD and MASH frequently coexist with obesity, hypertension, dyslipidemia and CKD and should be viewed within the broader cardiovascular-kidney-metabolic (CKM) framework. These conditions share common drivers, including insulin resistance, excess adiposity, endothelial dysfunction and chronic systemic inflammation. The relationship between MASLD and type 2 diabetes is bidirectional: Type 2 diabetes increases the risk for MASLD progression while MASLD may worsen insulin resistance and glycemic control. Consequently, MASLD, particularly when fibrosis is present, is associated not only with liver-related outcomes, including cirrhosis and hepatocellular carcinoma, but also with a higher risk for major adverse cardiovascular events. Management should therefore be coordinated, rather than organ-specific. Therapies selected to improve glycemic control and protect the heart and kidneys may also reduce liver fat or injury while newer liver-directed treatments can slow or potentially reverse fibrosis in patients with MASH. Viral hepatitis also warrants prompt treatment. Hepatitis C virus cure may improve glycemic control and is associated with reduced kidney and CV complications. A coordinated CKM-liver strategy can therefore address shared risk factors while improving outcomes across the liver, heart and kidneys. Weiner: Dialysis populations have historically experienced higher rates of HBV exposure. What enhanced infection control measures and monitoring situations should be implemented? Mohanty: Patients receiving hemodialysis remain at increased risk for HBV and HCV because of frequent blood exposure, shared treatment environments and impaired immune function, which increases susceptibility to infection and reduces vaccine responsiveness. Prevention should begin before admission to an outpatient dialysis unit. The CDC recommends baseline testing with hepatitis B surface antigen (HBsAg), antibody to hepatitis B surface antigen (anti-HBs) and antibody to hepatitis B core antigen (anti-HBc) for all patients starting hemodialysis. Susceptible patients should be vaccinated as early as possible, ideally before dialysis begins. For patients vaccinated after dialysis initiation, higher-dose regimens are recommended, followed by anti-HBs testing 1 to 2 months after series completion. Patients who do not achieve protective immunity should receive a second vaccine series. Ongoing HBV surveillance should be guided by immune status. Patients who remain susceptible should undergo monthly HBsAg testing, whereas vaccine responders should have anti-HBs levels checked annually and be revaccinated if titers fall below 10 IU/L. Patients with immunity from prior infection generally do not require additional routine HBV testing. In addition, patients should also be screened for HCV at dialysis initiation and every 6 months thereafter. Screening for viral hepatitis is also important when transferring to another dialysis center or changing treatment type, after a blood exposure, and whenever a new HBV case is identified in the unit. Infection control within the dialysis unit is equally important. Standard precautions, meticulous hand hygiene, appropriate use of personal protective equipment, safe medication and injection practices, disinfection of dialysis stations and shared equipment, and maintenance of water quality should be consistently enforced. Patients who test positive for HbsAg should be promptly isolated and dialyzed using dedicated equipment, with unit-specific exposure protocols implemented to reduce transmission. Weiner: How can hepatitis be prevented, detected and treated in the diabetes population? Which members of the healthcare team should support these efforts? Mohanty: Hepatitis prevention, detection and referral for treatment should be integrated into routine diabetes care. The key steps are to assess vaccination status, complete recommended screening and ensure timely follow-up of positive results. Prevention begins with HBV vaccination. The ADA Standards of Care recommend vaccination for adults with diabetes aged younger than 60 years and individualized vaccination for those aged 60 years or older. Vaccine response may be reduced by older age, obesity, CKD, immunosuppression and diabetes itself. The two-dose CpG-adjuvanted vaccine (Heplisav-B, Dynavax) provides higher seroprotection and a shorter completion interval than conventional three-dose regimens. Postvaccination serologic testing is important when confirmation of immunity is clinically necessary, including for patients receiving hemodialysis, immunocompromised individuals and healthcare personnel. Detection has been simplified by universal adult screening. All adults should undergo HBV screening at least once with HbsAg, anti-HBs and total anti-HBc, as well as one-time HCV antibody testing with reflex RNA testing. Repeat testing is indicated for those with ongoing risk. In diabetes care, the operational priority is to confirm that screening has been completed, document the results and establish a reliable follow-up pathway. Patients with positive results should be referred promptly. Chronic HBV can be effectively suppressed, and HCV is curable in most patients, including those with advanced CKD. HCV cure may also improve glycemic control and reduce kidney and CV complications. Antiviral selection and monitoring may intersect with renal function, lipid management and glucose-lowering therapy. The optimal model for hepatitis prevention and treatment often requires coordinated, interprofessional care, which depends on the clinical context and institutional resources. Primary care clinicians and endocrinologists can assess vaccination and screening status and initiate referrals while nurses, medical assistant and pharmacists can use standing orders, administer vaccines and track series completion. Diabetes care and education specialists can reinforce the relevance of hepatitis to diabetes outcomes; nephrology and dialysis teams can oversee dialysis-specific surveillance and infection control; and hepatology or infectious disease specialists can screen for hepatitis, manage confirmed infection and advanced liver disease. Each institution should establish a clear coordination point, assign responsibility for each step and embed hepatitis care within the diabetes pathway to prevent incomplete vaccination, missed results and unclosed referrals. Weiner: How can we improve prevention, detection and treatment in underserved and at-risk communities? Mohanty: Improving hepatitis prevention and care in underserved communities requires embedding services in the settings where people with diabetes already receive care, including primary care, endocrinology, pharmacies, dialysis units and diabetes education programs. Each setting should first identify its implementation gaps, such as low vaccination rates, incomplete screening or loss to follow-up, assess their magnitude and local causes, and tailor interventions accordingly. Strategies to improve vaccination and screening include standing vaccination orders, opt-out testing and electronic health record prompts, which can reduce missed opportunities and help address disparities. Testing should also be available beyond traditional clinics through community health centers, mobile programs, shelters, correctional facilities, syringe-service programs and other settings that reach people with limited access to care. Treatment access can be expanded through telehealth and task-shifting. Primary care clinicians, nurse practitioners and other trained nonspecialists can treat uncomplicated HCV with specialist support, allowing hepatology resources to focus on patients with cirrhosis, complex coinfection or treatment failure. Telehealth and Project ECHO (Extension for Community Healthcare Outcomes)-type models can extend expertise to rural and safety-net settings, although telephone-based options may be needed for patients with limited internet access. Finally, improving outcomes requires addressing barriers that commonly interrupt care, including transportation, insurance coverage, medication costs, housing instability, limited health literacy and stigma. Community health workers, peer navigators, social workers and culturally and linguistically tailored education can help patients move from screening to treatment without being lost to follow-up. The most effective approach is a coordinated, context-specific system that combines easier access, automated workflows, community partnerships and clear ownership of follow-up. Weiner: Is there current research available on other hepatitis treatments? Mohanty: Research in MASH treatment is advancing rapidly, an especially important development for people with type 2 diabetes who are at high risk for MASLD/MASH and resultant progressive liver fibrosis. Resmetirom (Rezdiffra, Madrigal Pharmaceuticals), a thyroid hormone receptor-beta agonist, and semaglutide (Wegovy, Novo Nordisk), a GLP-1 receptor agonist, are now FDA-approved for adults with noncirrhotic MASH and moderate to advanced fibrosis, in conjunction with lifestyle modification. Incretin-based therapies remain particularly relevant to diabetes care. Phase 2 trials of tirzepatide (Mounjaro/Zepbound, Eli Lilly) and survodutide (Boehringer Ingelheim) have shown improvements in MASH, weight and cardiometabolic risk. Fibroblast growth factor 21, or FGF21, analogs, including efruxifermin (Novo Nordisk) and pegozafermin (Roche), have also shown promising effects on liver fibrosis and metabolic parameters and are now being evaluated in phase 3 trials. These advances also reinforce the importance of identifying fibrosis early so eligible patients can benefit from approved therapies or clinical trials. For more information: Arpan Mohanty, M D , MSc, is a hepatologist at Boston Medical Center and assistant professor of medicine at Boston University Chobanian & Avedisian School of Medicine. Mohanty can be reached at endocrinology@healio.com . B 常规重要公共卫生新闻

加拿大葛兰素史克 (GSK) 获得贝匹罗韦森 (bepirovirsen) 监管提交的优先审查,贝匹罗韦生 (bepirovirsen) 是 慢性乙型肝炎 的潜在 功能性治愈 治疗 - 美通社PR-Newswire

GSK Canada secures priority review for regulatory submission of bepirovirsen, a potential functional cure treatment for chronic hepatitis B - PR Newswire Canada
新闻简报葛兰素史克加拿大公司已向加拿大监管机构提交了贝匹罗韦森(bepirovirsen)的新药申请,该药物是 慢性乙型肝炎 的潜在 功能性治愈 治疗。该提交得到了关键 III 期 B-Well 试验中具有统计学意义和临床意义的 功能性治愈 率的支持。慢性乙型肝炎 在加拿大仍然是一个重大的公共卫生问题,估计有 262,000 人患有这种疾病。它是全球肝癌的主要原因,约占肝细胞癌病例的 56%。目前的护理标准涉及终身核(酸)类似物治疗,功能性治愈 率约为 1%。Bepirovirsen 是一种在研反义寡核苷酸,在试验中显示出有希望的结果,其安全性和耐受性与之前的研究一致。然而,加拿大或任何其他国家尚未获得市场授权。提交的材料将根据加拿大卫生部的优先审查政策进行审查,数据计划在欧洲肝脏研究协会大会 (EASL) 上展示,并于今年晚些时候提交给同行评审期刊。
查看新闻五要素
时间
2026 年 4 月 30 日 /CNW/ - 葛兰素史克 (GSK) 今天宣布,已向加拿大提交贝匹罗韦森新药申请,供监管审查。该数据计划在欧洲肝脏研究协会大会 (EASL) 上展示,并于今年晚些时候提交给同行评审期刊。
地点与对象
加拿大,估计有 262,000 人患有 慢性乙型肝炎 (CHB)。
事件
葛兰素史克加拿大公司向加拿大监管机构提交了贝匹罗韦森(bepirovirsen)的新药申请,该药物是 慢性乙型肝炎 的潜在 功能性治愈 治疗。该提交得到了关键 III 期 B-Well 试验中具有统计学意义和临床意义的 功能性治愈 率的支持。
规模、影响与风险
慢性乙型肝炎 是加拿大的一个公共卫生问题,估计有 262,000 人患有 CHB。它是肝癌的主要原因,估计全球约 56% 的肝癌病例都是由它引起的。目前的护理标准要求终身 治疗,功能性治愈 率约为 1%。在所有排名终点中,与单独标准护理相比,贝匹罗韦森加标准护理产生了具有统计学意义和临床意义的 功能性治愈 率。这些试验证明了可接受的安全性和耐受性,与其他研究报告的结果一致。
应对、状态与不确定性
提交的材料将根据加拿大卫生部优先审查政策进行审查。贝匹罗韦森的安全性和有效性仍在调查中,加拿大或任何其他国家尚未获得市场授权。
B routine priorityPublic-health news

GSK Canada secures priority review for regulatory submission of bepirovirsen, a potential functional cure treatment for chronic hepatitis B - PR Newswire Canada

News Brief
GSK Canada has filed a new drug submission for bepirovirsen, a potential functional cure treatment for chronic hepatitis B, for regulatory review in Canada. The submission is supported by statistically significant and clinically meaningful functional cure rates from pivotal Phase III B-Well trials. Chronic hepatitis B remains a significant public health concern in Canada, with an estimated 262,000 people living with the condition. It is a leading cause of liver cancer globally, accounting for about 56% of hepatocellular carcinoma cases. The current standard of care involves lifelong nucleos(t)ide analogue therapy, with functional cure rates at approximately 1%. Bepirovirsen, an investigational antisense oligonucleotide, has shown promising results in trials, with a safety and tolerability profile consistent with prior studies. However, market authorization has not yet been granted in Canada or any other country. The submission will be reviewed under Health Canada's priority review policy, and the data is planned for presentation at the European Association for the Study of Liver Congress (EASL) and submission to a peer-reviewed journal later this year.
Original source text (English)

Sports Tourisme et Voyages Close submenu (Politique et intérêt public) Politique et intérêt public Politique et intérêt public Close submenu (Société) Société Société GSK Canada secures priority review for regulatory submission of bepirovirsen, a potential functional cure treatment for chronic hepatitis B Nouvelles fournies par GlaxoSmithKline Inc. 30 avr, 2026, 07:00 ET Partager cet article Partager cet article The new drug submission for bepirovirsen was granted priority review, underscoring the unmet need for patients Submission supported by statistically significant and clinically meaningful functional cure rates in pivotal Phase III B-Well trials An estimated 262,000 people in Canada live with chronic hepatitis B, a leading cause of liver cancer1 MISSISSAUGA, ON, April 30, 2026 /CNW/ - GSK today announced that it has filed a new drug submission for bepirovirsen for regulatory review in Canada. Bepirovirsen is an investigational antisense oligonucleotide (ASO) for the treatment of adults with chronic hepatitis B (CHB). The submission will be reviewed under the Health Canada priority review policy. Chronic hepatitis B remains a public health concern in Canada, with an estimated 262,000 people living with CHB.1 The current standard of care--nucleos(t)ide analogue therapy--typically requires lifelong treatment, and rates of functional cure remain very low, at approximately 1%.2 A functional cure is defined as undetectable hepatitis B virus DNA and hepatitis B surface antigen (HBsAg) in the blood for at least 24 weeks after stopping all treatment, indicating that the disease is controlled by the immune system without medication.3 Globally, CHB is estimated to cause roughly 56% of liver cancer cases. 4 "CHB imposes a significant burden on patients, increasing the risk of liver cancer and other serious liver disease. In Canada, these risks are exacerbated by inequities in screening, access to quantitative hepatitis B surface antigen testing, and connection to care," said Dr. Michelle Horn, Country Medical Director, GSK Canada. "The priority review of bepirovirsen is an important milestone in our efforts to transform care for people living with chronic hepatitis B." Positive Phase III results from the B‐Well 1 and B‐Well 2 trials support the regulatory submission for bepirovirsen. Bepirovirsen plus standard of care produced statistically significant and clinically meaningful functional cure rates versus standard of care alone across all ranked endpoints. The trials demonstrated an acceptable safety and tolerability profile consistent with what was reported in other studies. The data is planned for presentation at the European Association for the Study of Liver Congress (EASL) and submission to a peer‐reviewed journal later this year. The priority review designation in Canada reflects bepirovirsen's innovation and potential to address a disease with high unmet medical need. Bepirovirsen's safety and effectiveness are still under investigation, and market authorization has not yet been granted in Canada or any other country. The Canadian regulatory submission follows earlier filings in Japan, China, the European Union, and the United States. In the United States, bepirovirsen received Breakthrough Therapy designation from the Food and Drug Administration. About chronic hepatitis B Hepatitis B is a viral infection that can cause both acute and chronic liver disease. Chronic hepatitis B occurs when the immune system is unable to clear the virus, resulting in long-lasting infection that affects an estimated 262,000 people in Canada. The disease causes approximately 1.1 million deaths each year globally.1 Many patients require lifelong antiviral therapy for viral suppression, making functional cure a critical goal in disease management. The Canadian Association for the Study of the Liver (CASL) and the Association of Medical Microbiology and Infectious Disease Canada (AMMI) updated guidelines identify functional cure as the ultimate goal of treatment.5 About bepirovirsen Bepirovirsen is a triple action investigational antisense oligonucleotide (ASO), designed to recognise and orchestrate the destruction of the genetic components (i.e. mRNA and pregenomic RNA) of the hepatitis B virus that can lead to chronic disease. Bepirovirsen inhibits the replication of the viral genome in the body and suppresses the level of hepatitis B surface antigen (HBsAg) in the blood. Bepirovirsen also has innate immunostimulatory activity which may contribute to its therapeutic effect. About B-Well Clinical trial programmes B-Well 1 and B-Well 2 trials are global multi-centre, randomised, double-blind, placebo-controlled trials conducted in 29 countries. They assessed the efficacy, safety, pharmacokinetic profile, and the durability of functional cure in nucleos(t)ide analogue (NA)-treated participants with CHB and baseline surface antigen (HBsAg) ≤3000 IU/ml. The primary endpoint assessed the proportion of participants achieving functional cure in patients with baseline surface antigen (HBsAg) ≤3000 IU/ml. Functional cure was defined as sustained suppression (24 weeks or longer) of HBV DNA (<LLOQ) and HBsAg not detected, with or without hepatitis B surface antibodies, after a finite duration of therapy with bepirovirsen and off all HBV treatment. GSK licensed bepirovirsen from Ionis Pharmaceuticals and collaborated with them on its development. Bepirovirsen is currently not approved anywhere in the world. A breakthrough therapy designation (BTD) in the United States enables greater FDA guidance on an asset's development programme.6 The BTD for bepirovirsen builds on the Fast Track Designation also provided by the US FDA in February 2024. A Fast Track Designation expedites the review of drugs to treat serious conditions and fill an unmet medical need. About GSK GSK is a global biopharma company with a purpose to unite science, technology, and talent to get ahead of disease together. Find out more at www.gsk.ca. Cautionary statement regarding forward-looking statements GSK cautions investors that any forward-looking statements or projections made by GSK, including those made in this announcement, are subject to risks and uncertainties that may cause actual results to differ materially from those projected. Such factors include, but are not limited to, those described in the "Risk Factors" section in GSK's Annual Report on Form 20-F for 2025. B 常规重要公共卫生新闻

模型预测,乙型肝炎免疫计划的改变会增加感染率 - 哈佛医学院

Changes to Hepatitis B Immunization Schedule Would Increase Infections, Model Predicts - Harvard Medical School
新闻简报A study led by Harvard Medical School researchers at Massachusetts General Hospital warns that recent changes to the U.S. hepatitis B immunization schedule could lead to a significant rise in lifelong infections and a larger number of unprotected young adults. Previously, the CDC recommended universal birth vaccination for all newborns within 24 hours of birth, but in December 2025, the Advisory Committee on Immunization Practices (ACIP) shifted to a shared clinical decision-making approach for infants born to mothers who test negative for hepatitis B. The researchers modeled the impact of this change on one year’s worth of U.S. births, projecting that chronic infections could increase by 83 percent if half of families choose vaccination. Under the new approach, about 1.8 million young adults would reach age 20 without protection, compared to 232,000 under the universal vaccination strategy. The study, published in the Annals of Internal Medicine on October 20, 2026, highlights the potential for large clinical, public health, and economic effects due to increased childhood infections. The findings are based on computer modeling and depend on assumptions about vaccination rates, as no direct observations were made.
查看新闻五要素
时间
该研究于 2026 年 10 月 20 日发表,免疫接种计划的变更于 2025 年 12 月实施。模型模拟针对美国 20 岁以下出生婴儿一年的时间进行。
地点与对象
本研究聚焦于美国,具体针对乙肝检测阴性的母亲所生的婴儿,该人群包括大约3.6百万名婴儿。
事件
哈佛医学院马萨诸塞州总医院的研究人员对乙型肝炎免疫计划最近变化的影响进行了建模,该计划从普遍出生 疫苗接种 转变为针对某些婴儿的共享临床决策方法。该研究预测终身感染人数将会增加,未受保护的年轻人数量也会增加。
规模、影响与风险
Under the shared decision-making approach, chronic infections are projected to rise from 255 to 467, an 83 percent increase. Additionally, about 1.8 million young adults would reach age 20 unprotected against the virus, compared with 232,000 under universal birth vaccination. The study highlights the potential for significant clinical, public health, and economic effects due to increased childhood infections.
应对、状态与不确定性
研究结果是基于计算机模型的预测,并取决于对 疫苗接种 汇率的假设。该研究发表在《内科医学年鉴》上,其结果基于模型而非直接观察。研究团队包括传染病和全球健康方面的专家。
B routine priorityPublic-health news

Changes to Hepatitis B Immunization Schedule Would Increase Infections, Model Predicts - Harvard Medical School

News Brief
A study led by Harvard Medical School researchers at Massachusetts General Hospital warns that recent changes to the U.S. hepatitis B immunization schedule could lead to a significant rise in lifelong infections and a larger number of unprotected young adults. Previously, the CDC recommended universal birth vaccination for all newborns within 24 hours of birth, but in December 2025, the Advisory Committee on Immunization Practices (ACIP) shifted to a shared clinical decision-making approach for infants born to mothers who test negative for hepatitis B. The researchers modeled the impact of this change on one year’s worth of U.S. births, projecting that chronic infections could increase by 83 percent if half of families choose vaccination. Under the new approach, about 1.8 million young adults would reach age 20 without protection, compared to 232,000 under the universal vaccination strategy. The study, published in the Annals of Internal Medicine on October 20, 2026, highlights the potential for large clinical, public health, and economic effects due to increased childhood infections. The findings are based on computer modeling and depend on assumptions about vaccination rates, as no direct observations were made.

Get more HMS news Before December 2025, the Centers for Disease Control and Prevention (CDC) recommended vaccinating all newborns within 24 hours of birth. In December 2025, the Advisory Committee on Immunization Practices (ACIP) recommended that parents of infants born to mothers who test negative for hepatitis B decide when and whether to give the vaccine, including the birth dose. In January 2026, the CDC shifted to recommending vaccination only for certain high-risk groups, with a “shared clinical decision-making” approach for other infants. The research team modeled vaccination starting at age 2 months for those infants under both new approaches, simulating what would happen to one year’s worth of U.S. births — about 3.6 million babies — through age 20. Assuming half of families chose vaccination, chronic infections under the shared decision-making approach would rise from an estimated 255 to 467, an 83 percent increase. About 1.8 million young adults would reach age 20 unprotected against the virus, compared with 232,000 under universal birth vaccination. Infants and young children who contract hepatitis B are at high risk of lifelong infection, which can lead to cirrhosis and liver cancer. Even a small rise in childhood infections “could have large clinical, public health, and economic effects,” said lead and corresponding author Amir Mohareb, HMS assistant professor of medicine at Mass General and an infectious disease physician and clinician-researcher at the Center for Global Health at Mass General Brigham. The findings are projections from a computer model and depend on assumptions about how many families choose vaccination. They were published Oct. 20 in Annals of Internal Medicine. Read the full story from Mass General Brigham. Authorship, funding, disclosures Arthur Kim and Emily Hyle are co-senior authors on the study. Additional authors are Lakshmi Ganapathi, Caitlin Dugdale, Ruma Rajbhandari, Andrea Ciaranello, Anne Neilan, and Ravi Jhaveri. This work was supported in part by the National Institute of Allergy and Infectious Diseases (K01AI166126), a James and Audrey Foster MGH Research Scholar Award, and the Harvard University Center for AIDS Research (P30 AI060354), which is supported by the following National Institutes of Health (NIH) Co-Funding and Participating Institutes and Centers: NIAID; the National Cancer Institute; the Eunice Kennedy Shriver National Institute of Child Health and Human Development; the National Institute of Dental and Craniofacial Research; the National Heart, Lung, and Blood Institute; the National Institute on Drug Abuse; the National Institute of Mental Health; the National Institute on Aging; the National Institute of Diabetes and Digestive and Kidney Diseases; the National Institute of Nursing Research; the National Institute on Minority Health and Health Disparities; the Fogarty International Center; and the Office of AIDS Research. A full list of author disclosures can be found in the paper.
