乙型肝炎 疫苗:您的选择是否明智?

Hepatitis B vaccine: Is your choice an informed one?
新闻简报The article 'Hepatitis B vaccine: Is your choice an informed one?' published on November 5, 2026, by Bing News RSS discusses the implications of shifting from universal hepatitis B vaccination at birth to individualized decision-making for infants. It references a study in the Annals of Internal Medicine that models three prevention strategies: universal birth-dose vaccination, deferred vaccination, and shared decision-making. The study projects that under the universal strategy, 255 chronic HBV infections would occur among 3.6 million children by age 20, while shared decision-making could lead to 467 infections. The article also notes that delaying vaccination increases susceptibility to infection in adulthood, with 1.8 million individuals potentially at risk. The proposed changes to vaccination policy were stayed by a federal court, leaving the universal birth-dose recommendation in effect. The analysis highlights the importance of informed decision-making in the context of public health strategies and the potential consequences of policy shifts.
查看新闻五要素
时间
该文章发表于 2026 年 11 月 5 日。没有明确提及事件日期,但该研究对从出生到 20 岁的结果进行了建模,结果于 2024 年和 2026 年报告。
地点与对象
The analysis focuses on the United States, specifically on a cohort of 3.6 million American children, with implications for the general population.
事件
该文章根据发表在《内科医学年鉴》上的一项研究,讨论了将乙型肝炎 疫苗接种 策略从通用出生剂量 疫苗接种 改为个体化决策的影响。这是报告的分析和讨论,而不是确认的暴发。
规模、影响与风险
The study models three strategies for hepatitis B prevention. Under the universal birth-dose strategy, 255 chronic HBV infections are projected among 3.6 million children. Deferred vaccination increases this to 303, and shared decision-making raises it to 467. Additionally, the number of individuals entering adulthood susceptible to infection increases from 232,000 to 1.8 million under shared decision-making. The article highlights the potential consequences of these changes without providing specific case or death counts.
应对、状态与不确定性
该文章报告了一个模型及其含义,但没有明确提及当前的调查、测试或隔离措施。它讨论了政策变化及其潜在影响,但由于联邦法院中止审理,这些变化的状态仍未解决。该证据基于研究和政策建议,而不是基于已确认的 暴发 数据。
B routine priorityPublic-health news

Hepatitis B vaccine: Is your choice an informed one?

News Brief
The article 'Hepatitis B vaccine: Is your choice an informed one?' published on November 5, 2026, by Bing News RSS discusses the implications of shifting from universal hepatitis B vaccination at birth to individualized decision-making for infants. It references a study in the Annals of Internal Medicine that models three prevention strategies: universal birth-dose vaccination, deferred vaccination, and shared decision-making. The study projects that under the universal strategy, 255 chronic HBV infections would occur among 3.6 million children by age 20, while shared decision-making could lead to 467 infections. The article also notes that delaying vaccination increases susceptibility to infection in adulthood, with 1.8 million individuals potentially at risk. The proposed changes to vaccination policy were stayed by a federal court, leaving the universal birth-dose recommendation in effect. The analysis highlights the importance of informed decision-making in the context of public health strategies and the potential consequences of policy shifts.
Original source text (English)
Skip to main content Search search Link to Facebook page Link to X Link to YouTube channel Link to Instagram Link to flipboard Link to RSS Main navigation Home About About ACSH Financials Our Team Video Donate Publications Media/Contact Subscribe Write For Us Hepatitis B vaccine: Is your choice an informed one? email facebook twitter linkedin pocket reddit print By Chuck Dinerstein, MD, MBA — Nov 05, 2026 Related content: COVID Vaccine Trends: Too Little, Too Late, But Valuable COVID Vaccinations Should Be Distributed Through Schools Influenza Vaccination Saves Lives Yes, Vaccine Mandates Work: Really? Really! Nursing Homes, Shift Work and COVID Vaccinations A decision made in the first 24 hours of life may have consequences that remain invisible for decades. New research suggests that abandoning universal hepatitis B vaccination at birth could produce relatively few additional childhood infections while leaving millions more young adults vulnerable to a potentially lifelong disease. As vaccination becomes a shared decision, what does a parent need to know to share that decision equally with their physician? Image by ACSH using AI A Silent Infection With Lifelong Consequences Approximately two million Americans are living with chronic hepatitis B virus (HBV) infection, an illness without a cure, and, without treatment, 25% of infected individuals develop potentially life-threatening complications, including cirrhosis and liver cancer. HBV is diagnosed primarily from abnormal laboratory tests, not symptoms, driving us to see physicians, making it a stealthy disease. HBV is transmitted through exposure to infected blood and other body fluids, between adults and children in households and communities. Transmission from mother to infant during pregnancy or childbirth is especially concerning, as 90% of newborns without immune protection, infected perinatally, develop chronic HBV infection. Layering Protection, Closing the Gaps Universal screening during pregnancy began in 1988, followed by programs that provided hepatitis B immune globulin to at-risk newborns, universal infant vaccination in 1991, and a strengthened recommendation in 2018 that newborns receive their first vaccine dose within 24 hours of birth. As I have discussed previously, the rationale for this layered response comes from the “Swiss Cheese” model of protection: because all preventive measures have gaps (holes), multiple layers, like additional slices of Swiss cheese, each with its own holes, fill those gaps. As a result, perinatal and early-childhood HBV infections decreased from more than 25,000 annually in 1985 to fewer than 100 reported cases in 2024. When Universal Protection Becomes an Individual Choice In December 2025, federal vaccine advisers recommended replacing universal hepatitis B vaccination at birth with individualized decision-making for infants whose mothers tested negative for infection. A January 2026 federal revision moved further toward risk-based and shared clinical decision-making. However, a federal court stayed those changes in March, leaving the earlier universal birth-dose recommendation in effect. The proposed changes nevertheless raise an important question: What would happen if vaccination became a choice requiring individualized assessment rather than a routine safeguard? In the new era, where shared clinical decision-making might be rephrased as “make your own disease trajectory,” making an informed decision is crucial. A new study offers information for those choosing their own pathway. Three Strategies, Three Possible Futures The study reported in the Annals of Internal Medicine models three strategic approaches to prevention and is based on disease trajectory data. [1] It follows a hypothetical 3.6 million American children from birth through age 20. The principal outcomes were the number of children developing chronic hepatitis B and the number entering adulthood susceptible to infection. One further note: this is a model, and its conclusions depend on assumptions about vaccination uptake and transmission risk, which we will consider shortly. The three strategies were: Universal birth-dose vaccination: The older recommendation that all newborns receive their first hepatitis B vaccine dose within 24 hours of birth, regardless of maternal infection status. The model assumes 91.1% complete the vaccine series by 24 months. Deferred vaccination: A component of the newer recommendation in which infants begin vaccination at 2 months rather than at birth, delaying protection. The model assumes 87.1% complete the series. Shared decision-making: The second component of the newer recommendation, in which vaccination is no longer universally recommended and instead depends on discussions between parents and clinicians or on identification of elevated risk. The model assumes only 50% complete vaccination. In all three strategies, vaccination at birth remains recommended for infants whose mothers have hepatitis B or whose infection status is unknown. Small Numbers, Large Consequences Under the former universal birth-dose strategy, the model projects 255 chronic HBV infections among 3.6 million children followed through age 20. Delaying vaccination until two months increases that number to 303, while shared decision-making raises it to 467. The more striking difference is in those entering adulthood still susceptible to infection: 232,000 with universal birth-dose vaccination, 474,000 with delayed vaccination, and 1.8 million with shared decision-making. Stay curious. Stay informed. Free, independent writing delivered weekly — no ads, no sponsored fluff. Join Now Donate Horizontal infections refer to cases in which another household member, or the mother after the perinatal period, was the source. In each instance, because we continue to protect the children most at risk, those born to known HBV-infected mothers have perinatally acquired infections that remain essentially unchanged. The increases in HBV infections are driven by horizontal infection and by the decision not to vaccinate at all. While the number of infected individuals remains small, at less than 0.05% of the entire 3.6 million cohort, susceptibility rises steadily under each strategy until it reaches slightly more than 50% of that cohort. Although susceptibility is not infection, this increasingly large, unprotected population becomes a source of adult HBV infections that can generate more infected individuals. The take-away message is that the newer recommendations yield a modest absolute increase in infections. Still, the long-term impacts, which may seem less salient at the time of decision-making, can be substantial. What Happens When the Assumptions Change? Here is the data for scenarios in which vaccination completion or newborn vaccination rates fall, maternal prevalence and horizontal exposure risk rise, and vaccination is deferred for the first two months of life. The study's most consequential public health finding may be the distinction between the relatively small absolute number of additional childhood infections and the much larger population entering adulthood unprotected. This additional data reinforces two drivers of infection in the model. Delaying vaccination increases risk through two reinforcing mechanisms: it prolongs the period during which children remain vulnerable to infection through horizontal, household, and community exposure, and later vaccination also reduces the likelihood of completing the three-dose series, leaving more individuals susceptible into adulthood. Second, when declining vaccination extends to infants whose mothers are infected or whose infection status is unknown, the model projects nearly three times as many infections acquired at birth compared with universal birth-dose vaccination. The key distinction is that delaying vaccination mainly lengthens the period without protection, whereas replacing universal vaccination with shared decision-making may leave a substantial proportion of children unvaccinated altogether. The results, especially those around shared decision-making, depend heavily on assumptions. The investigators estimated that vaccine completion would decline to 50% because it is too early to measure outcomes after implementation. The researchers also assumed that the monthly probability of horizontal HBV exposure varied by age, ranging from 25 to 300 exposures per 100,000 individuals per month. Much of the projected increase in susceptibility reflects these assumptions. Two other studies strengthen the “direction” of their conclusions, though they do not fully replicate the model’s quantification. Does Missing the First Dose Mean Missing the Rest? A retrospective study published in JAMA Network Open examined more than one million children in the Vaccine Safety Datalink, which includes electronic health records from nine geographically separate sites, focusing on those who received the hepatitis B vaccine. Children were divided into two groups based on whether they received the hepatitis B vaccine within three days of birth, with the outcome being completion of the full three-dose vaccination series by 18 months. Among those receiving a birth dose, 97% completed the full series by 18 months and were therefore the most fully protected. Of the remaining almost 19%, only 67.5% completed the series within 18 months, and slightly more than a quarter had no vaccination at all. The study examined results from 2014 to 2023. While the percentage fully vaccinated in the birth-dose cohort remained unchanged, completion fell in the delayed-dose group, with only 55% of the 2023 cohort fully immunized by 18 months. In the real world, the association between the birth dose and series completion is substantial, and delaying vaccination increased the rate of incomplete treatment even before the new guidelines were promulgated. The JAMA data support the model’s estimate of completed doses among birth-dose recipients and suggest the model's deferred vaccination completion rate was overly optimistic: the model posited 87% completion, while the real-world rate was 67.5%. Finally, the 55% of the 2023 cohort that delayed vaccination but completed the series lends some support to the model’s 50% assumption. On balance, the JAMA study strengthens the model’s directional conclusions while introducing uncertainty about the magnitude of its predictions. The Risks Beyond the Birth Mother One of the model's most striking findings is the role of horizontal transmission. Under universal birth-dose vaccination, approximately 12% of projected chronic childhood infections result from horizontal transmission. With shared decision-making, that proportion increases to approximately 50%. In absolute terms, the model projects nearly eight times as many horizontally acquired chronic infections under shared decision-making compared with universal birth-dose vaccination. The investigators explicitly acknowledge that horizontal transmission is one of their model's greatest uncertainties. For real-world data, we turn to an abstract from a recent infectious disease conference from Kaiser Permanente’s research division. Their data come from a retrospective review of health records that documented diagnoses of horizontal sources within a household, not measured transmission. Kaiser identified approximately 1,280 per 100,000 children living with an infected household member at any one point in time. While household prevalence is not exposure events, those numbers certainly suggest that the model’s horizontal exposure risk from 25 to 300 per 100,000 children is biologically plausible. The findings strengthen the argument that hepatitis B prevention cannot rely exclusively on identifying infected mothers. What About the Risks of Vaccination? The CDC identifies injection-site soreness, fatigue, and fever among recognized adverse effects. Serious allergic reactions are rare, and available safety monitoring has not identified unexpected major safety concerns. A 2026 review in Pediatrics found strong evidence for the safety and effectiveness of birth-dose vaccination and no demonstrated safety advantage to delaying vaccination. An Informed Choice Requires More Than Permission to Choose Informed consent is more than asking parents whether they want their newborn vaccinated. It requires giving them the information needed to weigh the benefits, risks, and uncertainties of their decision. For hepatitis B, the immediate risk of infection for most newborns is small, particularly when their mothers have tested negative. The vaccine has a long record of safety and effectiveness, although, like any medical intervention, it is not entirely without risk. What is less predictable is a child's exposure to hepatitis B over the next several decades, or whether postponing the first dose will mean never completing the series. Parents should be free to ask questions, challenge recommendations, and participate meaningfully in medical decisions affecting their children. But choice without an understanding of its consequences is not truly informed consent. If we expect parents to make these decisions, we owe them more than reassurance or rhetoric. We owe them the evidence, including what we know, what we do not know, and what their children may have to live with. [1] It incorporated five possible states of the disease: newborn, susceptible to hepatitis B, immune through vaccination, recovered from a previous infection, and chronically infected; and perinatal infection from mother to infant around childbirth, and horizontal transmission through subsequent household or community exposure. B 常规重要公共卫生新闻

Hibsago(贝匹罗韦森)在加拿大获批,成为第一个也是唯一一个可实现 功能性治愈 或 慢性乙型肝炎 的有限疗法 - BioSpace

Hibsago (bepirovirsen) approved in Canada as the first and only finite therapy indicated to achieve functional cure of chronic hepatitis B - BioSpace
新闻简报On November 3, 2026, Health Canada approved Hibsago (bepirovirsen) as the first and only finite therapy for achieving a functional cure of chronic hepatitis B (CHB) in non-cirrhotic adult patients who have been on nucleos(t)ide analogue therapy for at least 6 months and have HBV DNA <90 IU/mL and HBsAg levels ≤3,000 IU/mL. The approval is based on data from Phase III B-Well trials, which showed significant functional cure rates of 19% and 26% in patients with baseline HBsAg levels of ≤3000 IU/mL and ≤1000 IU/mL, respectively, compared to 0% in the placebo group. CHB is a serious, progressive liver disease affecting an estimated 262,000 people in Canada and causing approximately 1.1 million deaths globally each year. The therapy, an antisense oligonucleotide (ASO), reduces viral RNA and HBsAg levels, potentially allowing the immune system to regain control. Hibsago's approval follows authorizations in Japan and the United States, with supply expected in Canada by early 2027. The Canadian Association for the Study of the Liver (CASL) and the Association of Medical Microbiology and Infectious Disease Canada (AMMI) updated guidelines to identify functional cure as the optimal treatment goal.
查看新闻五要素
时间
基线 HBsAg 水平较低时,临床疗效较高,尤其是基线 HBsAg 水平≤1,000 IU/mL 的患者。
地点与对象
加拿大估计有 262,000 人患有 慢性乙型肝炎。
事件
加拿大卫生部批准 Hibsago(贝匹罗韦森)作为第一个也是唯一一个在接受核苷(酸)类似物治疗至少 6 个月且 HBV DNA <90 IU/mL、HBsAg 水平≤3,000 IU/mL 的非肝硬化成年患者中达到 功能性治愈 或 慢性乙型肝炎 (CHB) 的有限疗法。
规模、影响与风险
功能性治愈 在基线 HBsAg 水平 ≤3000 IU/ml 的成人中显示出 19% 的疗效,在基线 HBsAg 水平 ≤1000 IU/ml 的成人中显示出 26% 的疗效,与安慰剂组的 0% 相比。慢性乙型肝炎是一种严重的、进行性的肝病,估计影响加拿大 262,000 人,每年在全球范围内导致约 1.1 百万死亡。
应对、状态与不确定性
Hibsago 在加拿大的批准是根据加拿大卫生部的优先审查政策获得的,反映了其创新性和解决医疗需求未得到满足的疾病的潜力。该批准是在日本和美国获得授权之后获得的。预计将于 2027 年初在加拿大向符合条件的患者提供供应。
B routine priorityPublic-health news

Hibsago (bepirovirsen) approved in Canada as the first and only finite therapy indicated to achieve functional cure of chronic hepatitis B - BioSpace

News Brief
On November 3, 2026, Health Canada approved Hibsago (bepirovirsen) as the first and only finite therapy for achieving a functional cure of chronic hepatitis B (CHB) in non-cirrhotic adult patients who have been on nucleos(t)ide analogue therapy for at least 6 months and have HBV DNA <90 IU/mL and HBsAg levels ≤3,000 IU/mL. The approval is based on data from Phase III B-Well trials, which showed significant functional cure rates of 19% and 26% in patients with baseline HBsAg levels of ≤3000 IU/mL and ≤1000 IU/mL, respectively, compared to 0% in the placebo group. CHB is a serious, progressive liver disease affecting an estimated 262,000 people in Canada and causing approximately 1.1 million deaths globally each year. The therapy, an antisense oligonucleotide (ASO), reduces viral RNA and HBsAg levels, potentially allowing the immune system to regain control. Hibsago's approval follows authorizations in Japan and the United States, with supply expected in Canada by early 2027. The Canadian Association for the Study of the Liver (CASL) and the Association of Medical Microbiology and Infectious Disease Canada (AMMI) updated guidelines to identify functional cure as the optimal treatment goal.
Original source text (English)
Press Releases Hibsago (bepirovirsen) approved in Canada as the first and only finite therapy indicated to achieve functional cure of chronic hepatitis B November 3, 2026 | 6 min read Twitter LinkedIn Facebook Email Print Hibsago was reviewed under Health Canada's Priority Review policy, reflecting its innovation and potential to address a disease with high unmet medical need Approval is based on data from Phase III B-Well trials which demonstrated significant and clinically meaningful functional cure rates An estimated 262,000 people in Canada live with chronic hepatitis B 1 , a leading cause of liver cancer 2 MISSISSAUGA, ON , Nov. 3, 2026 /CNW/ -- GSK announced today that Health Canada has approved Hibsago (bepirovirsen) as a finite treatment to achieve functional cure of chronic hepatitis B (CHB) virus infection in non-cirrhotic adult patients who have been on nucleos(t)ide analogue therapy for at least 6 months and with HBV DNA <90 IU/mL, and have hepatitis B surface antigen (HBsAg) levels ≤3,000 IU/mL. Clinical efficacy is higher with lower baseline HBsAg levels, especially in patients with baseline HBsAg levels ≤1,000 IU/mL. 3 Hibsago is an antisense oligonucleotide (ASO) and the first and only therapy in Canada with an indication to achieve functional cure in CHB . " Hibsago's authorization in Canada represents an important step forward for people living with CHB, a serious, progressive liver disease," says Dr. Michelle Horn, Country Medical Director, GSK Canada. "The possibility of a functional cure delivered through a finite course of therapy changes expectations from lifelong management to the prospect of stopping treatment and supporting long-term liver health. CHB remains an area of high unmet need, and GSK remains committed to advancing hepatology to help address this gap for Canadians living with this disease." Approval in Canada was supported by results from the B-Well phase III trials showing unprecedented functional cure rates of 19% in adults with ≤3000 IU/ml baseline HBsAg and 26% in adults with baseline HBsAg ≤1000 IU/ml, compared with 0% functional cure in the placebo (standard-of-care alone) group. 4 Functional cure occurs when the hepatitis B virus DNA and viral protein – HBsAg – are undetectable for at least 24 weeks after stopping all hepatitis B treatment. This indicates the disease is controlled by the immune system without medication. "For many people in Canada living with CHB, the diagnosis comes with years of worry about liver damage and the impact on work, family and future plans, and it can still carry stigma and misunderstanding in their communities," added Jennifer Nebesky, CEO, Liver Canada. "New options like Hibsago can offer people living with CHB greater confidence about their liver health and help us tackle the ongoing challenges that still affect the CHB community across Canada." CHB is a long-term viral infection, which affects an estimated 262,000 people in Canada. 1 It often goes undetected for years because many people have few or no symptoms, but over time it can lead to serious liver damage, including cirrhosis and liver cancer. 2 Hibsago's approval in Canada was granted under Health Canada's Priority Review policy, reflecting its innovation and potential to address a disease with high unmet medical need. It follows authorizations in Japan and the United States. Hibsago supply is anticipated to become available in Canada to eligible patients in early 2027. About chronic hepatitis B Hepatitis B is a viral infection that can cause both acute and chronic liver disease. Chronic hepatitis B occurs when the immune system is unable to clear the virus, resulting in long-lasting infection that affects an estimated 262,000 people in Canada. The disease causes approximately 1.1 million deaths each year globally. 1 In people with chronic hepatitis B, a loss in hepatitis B surface antigen is associated with up to an 89% reduction in risk of liver cancer and a 62% reduction in risk of all-cause mortality. 5 Many patients require lifelong antiviral therapy for viral suppression, making functional cure a critical goal in disease management. The Canadian Association for the Study of the Liver (CASL) and the Association of Medical Microbiology and Infectious Disease Canada (AMMI) updated guidelines identify functional cure as the optimal goal of treatment. 6 About Hibsago (bepirovirsen) Hibsago (bepirovirsen) is an antisense oligonucleotide (ASO) designed to recognise and inhibit the production of the genetic components (i.e. RNA) of the hepatitis B virus that can lead to chronic disease, potentially allowing a person's immune system to regain control. Hibsago reduces the production of RNA and viral proteins associated with HBV, suppresses the level of hepatitis B surface antigen (HBsAg) in the blood, and stimulates the immune system to increase the chances of a durable and sustained response. GSK licensed Hibsago (bepirovirsen) from Ionis Pharmaceuticals and collaborated with them on its development. Please consult the Hibsago product monograph at gsk.ca for complete safety information. The product monograph is also available by calling 1-800-387-7374. About B-Well Clinical trial programs B-Well 1 and B-Well 2 trials are global multi-centre, randomised, double-blind, placebo-controlled trials conducted in 29 countries. They assessed the efficacy, safety, pharmacokinetic profile, and the durability of functional cure in nucleos(t)ide analogue (NA)-treated participants with CHB and baseline surface antigen (HBsAg) ≤3000 IU/ml. The primary endpoint assessed the proportion of participants achieving functional cure in patients with baseline surface antigen (HBsAg) ≤3000 IU/ml. Functional cure in patients with baseline HBsAg ≤1000 IU/ml was a key secondary endpoint. Functional cure was defined as sustained suppression (24 weeks or longer) of HBV DNA (<LLOQ) and HBsAg not detected, with or without hepatitis B surface antibodies, after a finite duration of therapy with bepirovirsen and off all HBV treatment. About GSK GSK is a global biopharma company with a purpose to unite science, technology, and talent to get ahead of disease together. Find out more at www.gsk.ca . Cautionary statement regarding forward-looking statements GSK cautions investors that any forward-looking statements or projections made by GSK, including those made in this announcement, are subject to risks and uncertainties that may cause actual results to differ materially from those projected. Such factors include, but are not limited to, those described in the "Risk Factors" section in GSK's Annual Report on Form 20-F for 2025, and GSK's Q3 Results for 2026. B 常规重要公共卫生新闻

医生对终止乙型肝炎出生剂量的警告集中在筛查差距上,这可能会使新生儿得不到保护 - 自然世界新闻

Doctors' Alarm Over Ending the Hepatitis B Birth Dose Centers on a Screening Gap That Could Leave Newborns Unprotected - Nature World News
新闻简报Doctors and medical professionals are raising concerns over the potential end of the universal hepatitis B birth dose recommendation in the United States, highlighting a critical screening gap that could leave newborns vulnerable. The CDC's Advisory Committee on Immunization Practices voted in December 2025 to stop recommending the universal birth dose, but the change has not taken effect due to a federal court stay issued on March 16, 2026. The Annals of Internal Medicine study, published on Oct. 19, 2026, projects a significant rise in chronic hepatitis B infections if the universal recommendation is replaced, with the largest increases under shared decision-making. The study suggests that delaying the first dose to 2 months of age could raise chronic infections by 19%, while shared decision-making could lead to an 83% increase. The modeling also estimates that about 1.8 million young adults could be susceptible to hepatitis B by age 20 under the new approach, compared to fewer than 250,000 under the universal strategy. The risk of chronic infection is high for infants infected during early childhood, with over 90% of cases becoming chronic. The study does not directly estimate liver cancer or death rates but underscores the potential long-term health consequences. The change remains under legal review, with no ruling reported as of Oct. 6, 2026.
查看新闻五要素
时间
The article was published on Nov 03 2026, 2:47 AM EST. The modeling study was published on Oct 19, 2026, and the court status was reported through Oct. The federal court stayed the change on March 16, 2026.
地点与对象
该事件涉及美国,特别是拟议的乙型肝炎出生剂量建议变更对新生儿及其母亲的影响。受影响的人群包括孕妇及其新生儿,重点是那些可能未接受乙型肝炎检测或可能得到不正确或延迟结果的人。
事件
医生和医疗专业人士对通用乙型肝炎出生剂量建议可能终止表示担忧,称筛查差距可能导致新生儿得不到保护。这项变更由 CDC 免疫实践咨询委员会于 2025 年 12 月提出,允许父母和医生决定是否为乙型肝炎检测呈阴性的母亲的婴儿注射出生疫苗。由于联邦法院于 2026 年 3 月 16 日发布暂缓令,该决定目前已暂停。
规模、影响与风险
The Annals of Internal Medicine study projects a 19% increase in chronic hepatitis B infections among U.S. children if the universal birth dose is replaced, with an even higher 83% increase under shared decision-making. The model also estimates that about 1.8 million young adults could be susceptible to hepatitis B under shared decision-making by age 20, compared to fewer than 250,000 under the universal birth dose. The risk of chronic infection is particularly high for infants infected during early childhood, with over 90% of cases becoming chronic.
应对、状态与不确定性
由于联邦法院暂缓执行,拟议的乙型肝炎出生剂量建议变更目前已暂停。CDC 已接受这一更改,但尚未生效。模型研究和相关分析正在进行中,截至 10 月尚未报告任何裁决。研究结果基于预测和假设,文章指出这些是模型,而不是观察到的结果。

Doctors' Alarm Over Ending the Hepatitis B Birth Dose Centers on a Screening Gap That Could Leave Newborns Unprotected - Nature World News

News Brief
Doctors and medical professionals are raising concerns over the potential end of the universal hepatitis B birth dose recommendation in the United States, highlighting a critical screening gap that could leave newborns vulnerable. The CDC's Advisory Committee on Immunization Practices voted in December 2025 to stop recommending the universal birth dose, but the change has not taken effect due to a federal court stay issued on March 16, 2026. The Annals of Internal Medicine study, published on Oct. 19, 2026, projects a significant rise in chronic hepatitis B infections if the universal recommendation is replaced, with the largest increases under shared decision-making. The study suggests that delaying the first dose to 2 months of age could raise chronic infections by 19%, while shared decision-making could lead to an 83% increase. The modeling also estimates that about 1.8 million young adults could be susceptible to hepatitis B by age 20 under the new approach, compared to fewer than 250,000 under the universal strategy. The risk of chronic infection is high for infants infected during early childhood, with over 90% of cases becoming chronic. The study does not directly estimate liver cancer or death rates but underscores the potential long-term health consequences. The change remains under legal review, with no ruling reported as of Oct. 6, 2026.
Original source text (English)
Home Animals Biology Environment Health & Medicine Tech Travel Space Natureworldnews.com Let's Connect Trending climate change Earth whales Doctors’ Alarm Over Ending the Hepatitis B Birth Dose Centers on a Screening Gap That Could Leave Newborns Unprotected By Lucas Caldwell Published: Nov 03 2026, 2:47 AM EST Facebook Twitter Linkedin Comment Mail FRED TANNEAU | AFP via Getty Images Update Note: This article reports new analysis involving the federal hepatitis B birth-dose change following Nature World News' earlier coverage of the state lawsuit and modeling study. Latest verified information: Annals of Internal Medicine study published Oct. 19, 2026; Live Science report published Oct. 27, 2026; court status as reported through Oct. 29, 2026. The fight over the hepatitis B birth dose often sounds like a debate about choice. Doctors who oppose ending the universal shot say it is really about something else — a safety net. Even when the system works as designed, some mothers are never tested, some results are wrong or arrive late, and some babies are exposed at home after birth. The birth dose, given within 24 hours of delivery, is meant to protect newborns in all of those cases. That concern explains why a new modeling study in the Annals of Internal Medicine has drawn so much attention. It projects sharply more chronic infections among U.S. children if the universal birth dose is replaced, with the largest increases when the decision is left to individual families and providers. For expectant parents, the practical status is clear for now. A federal court paused the change in March, so the universal birth-dose recommendation remains in effect while the legal fight continues. What the Proposed Change Would Do Since 1991, the CDC has recommended that all medically stable newborns receive a first dose of hepatitis B vaccine shortly after birth. In December 2025, the CDC's Advisory Committee on Immunization Practices voted to end that universal recommendation, and the CDC accepted it. Under the new approach, babies of mothers who test positive for hepatitis B, or whose status is unknown, would still get the birth dose. For mothers who test negative, parents and doctors would decide together whether to give it, which the CDC calls shared clinical decision-making. Babies who skip the birth dose would start the series no earlier than 2 months of age. That is the strongest argument for the change. Supporters, including former acting CDC Director Jim O'Neill, have said newborns of mothers who test negative face little risk, and the Department of Health and Human Services has framed the policy as respecting parental choice. The change has not taken effect. On March 16, 2026, a federal judge in Massachusetts stayed it, along with the reconstituted committee's other votes. Nature World News previously reported on the widening state lawsuit and the modeling study, noting that the federal appeal was argued Oct. 6 with no ruling reported as of Oct. 29. Why the Screening Net Has Holes The science behind the alarm is about age. When hepatitis B infection happens in infancy or early childhood, more than 90% of cases become chronic, according to Live Science's summary of the research. Among people with chronic infection, about 15% to 25% go on to develop cirrhosis, liver cancer, or other chronic liver disease, usually years or decades later. Prenatal screening is supposed to catch infected mothers, but it is not universal. About 86% of pregnant people are currently tested for hepatitis B, STAT reported. History also suggests that weakening the general recommendation can reduce vaccination even for babies who are supposed to get it. When the birth dose was paused for babies of negative mothers in 1999, the share of babies born to unscreened mothers who got the vaccine at birth fell from 53% to 7%, even though the advice for those babies had not changed. "Something can be missed at any step in the care process, so the vaccine dose at birth provides protection," said Dr. Amir Mohareb, an infectious-diseases physician at Massachusetts General Hospital and Harvard Medical School and the study's corresponding author. Grace Lee, a former committee chair at Stanford University School of Medicine, put the concern more bluntly in an editorial accompanying separate studies: "implementation is not about intent, it is about friction." What the Models Project The Annals study simulated babies born in 2026 through age 20, running the model 1,000 times with uncertain inputs varied. Delaying the first dose to 2 months raised chronic infections by 19% compared with the universal birth dose. Under shared decision-making, where coverage by age 2 was assumed to drop to about 50%, chronic infections rose 83%. The model also projected about 1.8 million young adults a year reaching age 20 still susceptible to hepatitis B under shared decision-making, compared with fewer than 250,000 under the universal birth dose. In every simulation, the birth-dose strategy produced the fewest chronic infections. Two earlier studies in JAMA Pediatrics reached similar conclusions using different methods. One projected about 76 additional chronic infections and 29 hepatitis B-related deaths per birth cohort from delaying the first dose, plus more than $16 million in added health care costs. These are models — not observed outcomes. Their results depend on assumptions about screening, vaccine uptake and household exposure. The Annals study did not estimate liver cancer cases or deaths directly. Dr. Norah Terrault, a transplant hepatologist at the USC Keck School of Medicine who was not involved, said it "presents an accurate projection of how the vaccine recommendations may impact future chronic HBV in the U.S." Questions for Expectant Parents Parents expecting a baby can ask their obstetrician or midwife whether they have been tested for hepatitis B during pregnancy and what the result was. They can also tell the hospital in advance whether they want the birth dose given, since it is offered in the first 24 hours. The American Academy of Pediatrics continues to recommend the birth dose for every medically stable newborn, and insurers have said they will keep covering it. Babies born to mothers who test positive should receive both the vaccine and hepatitis B immune globulin within 12 hours of birth. Anyone with questions about their own infection status, or whether household members have been tested, should talk with a doctor. Hepatitis B often causes no symptoms for years, which is why testing matters. The bottom line: the universal hepatitis B birth dose remains the operative recommendation while courts review the change. Doctors' central worry is that real-world gaps in screening would leave some newborns exposed, and multiple models project more lifelong infections if the safety net is removed. Related Coalition of 16 Attorneys General Widens Lawsuit Over CDC's Hepatitis B Change as a Model Projects a Sharp Rise in Childhood Infections A Common Gut Bacterium Makes a Cholesterol-Lowering Compound That Shrank Artery Plaque in Mice Marine Molecule Kills Brain-Eating Amoeba Faster Than the Standard Drug in Lab Tests, UC San Diego Scientists Report What Readers Want to Know Is the hepatitis B birth dose still recommended? Yes. A March 16, 2026, federal court order paused the change, so the universal birth-dose recommendation remains in effect. What would the change do? Babies of mothers who test negative would receive the birth dose only after shared decision-making, and those who skip it would start the series at 2 months or later. Why does infection in infancy matter so much? More than 90% of hepatitis B infections acquired in infancy or early childhood become chronic, raising later risks of cirrhosis and liver cancer. Why isn't a negative prenatal test enough? Not every pregnant person is tested, results can be missed or wrong, and babies can be exposed after birth by infected household members. Are the projected increases certain? No. They come from models that depend on assumptions about screening and vaccine uptake. What should expectant parents do? Confirm your prenatal hepatitis B test result with your provider and discuss the birth dose with your obstetrician or pediatrician before delivery. © 2026 NatureWorldNews.com All rights reserved. Do not reproduce without permission. The proposed change in hepatitis B birth-dose recommendations is currently paused due to a federal court stay. The CDC has accepted the change, but it has not taken effect. The modeling study and related analyses are ongoing, with no ruling reported as of Oct. Hibsago(贝匹罗韦森)在加拿大获批,成为第一个也是唯一一个可实现 功能性治愈 或 慢性乙型肝炎 的有限疗法 - 雅虎财经

Hibsago (bepirovirsen) approved in Canada as the first and only finite therapy indicated to achieve functional cure of chronic hepatitis B - Yahoo Finance
新闻简报2026 年 11 月 3 日,加拿大卫生部批准 Hibsago(贝匹罗韦森)作为第一个也是唯一一个在非肝硬化成年患者中实现 功能性治愈 或 慢性乙型肝炎 (CHB) 的有限疗法。此次批准基于 III 期 B-Well 试验的数据,该试验表明,在基线 HBsAg 水平≤3000 IU/mL 和≤1000 IU/mL 的成人中,功能性治愈 的显着率分别为 19% 和 26%。这些比率与安慰剂组的 0% 进行比较。该疗法适用于已接受核苷(酸)类似物治疗至少 6 个月且 HBV DNA 水平 <90 IU/mL 的患者。此次批准反映了加拿大 CHB 治疗 的医疗需求尚未得到满足,估计加拿大有 262,000 人患有这种疾病。这些试验是全球性的、多中心的、随机的、双盲的、安慰剂对照的,在 29 个国家进行。这一决定标志着 CHB 治疗 的重大进步,为受影响的个体提供了潜在的治疗方法。

规模、影响与风险

B routine priorityPublic-health news

Hibsago (bepirovirsen) approved in Canada as the first and only finite therapy indicated to achieve functional cure of chronic hepatitis B - Yahoo Finance

News Brief
On November 3, 2026, Health Canada approved Hibsago (bepirovirsen) as the first and only finite therapy for achieving a functional cure of chronic hepatitis B (CHB) in non-cirrhotic adult patients. This approval was based on data from the Phase III B-Well trials, which demonstrated significant functional cure rates of 19% and 26% in adults with baseline HBsAg levels of ≤3000 IU/mL and ≤1000 IU/mL, respectively. These rates were compared to 0% in the placebo group. The therapy is intended for patients who have been on nucleos(t)ide analogue therapy for at least 6 months and have HBV DNA levels <90 IU/mL. The approval reflects the high unmet medical need for CHB treatment in Canada, where an estimated 262,000 people live with the condition. The trials were global, multi-centre, randomised, double-blind, and placebo-controlled, conducted in 29 countries. This decision marks a significant advancement in the treatment of CHB, offering a potential cure for affected individuals.

Hibsago (bepirovirsen) approved in Canada as the first and only finite therapy indicated to achieve functional cure of chronic hepatitis B CNW Group Tue, November 3, 2026 at 6:00 AM CST 6 min read Hibsago was reviewed under Health Canada's Priority Review policy, reflecting its innovation and potential to address a disease with high unmet medical need Approval is based on data from Phase III B-Well trials which demonstrated significant and clinically meaningful functional cure rates An estimated 262,000 people in Canada live with chronic hepatitis B1, a leading cause of liver cancer2 MISSISSAUGA, ON, Nov. 3, 2026 /CNW/ -- GSK announced today that Health Canada has approved Hibsago (bepirovirsen) as a finite treatment to achieve functional cure of chronic hepatitis B (CHB) virus infection in non-cirrhotic adult patients who have been on nucleos(t)ide analogue therapy for at least 6 months and with HBV DNA <90 IU/mL, and have hepatitis B surface antigen (HBsAg) levels ≤3,000 IU/mL. Clinical efficacy is higher with lower baseline HBsAg levels, especially in patients with baseline HBsAg levels ≤1,000 IU/mL.3 Hibsago is an antisense oligonucleotide (ASO) and the first and only therapy in Canada with an indication to achieve functional cure in CHB. GSK logo "Hibsago's authorization in Canada represents an important step forward for people living with CHB, a serious, progressive liver disease," says Dr. Michelle Horn, Country Medical Director, GSK Canada. "The possibility of a functional cure delivered through a finite course of therapy changes expectations from lifelong management to the prospect of stopping treatment and supporting long-term liver health. CHB remains an area of high unmet need, and GSK remains committed to advancing hepatology to help address this gap for Canadians living with this disease." Approval in Canada was supported by results from the B-Well phase III trials showing unprecedented functional cure rates of 19% in adults with ≤3000 IU/ml baseline HBsAg and 26% in adults with baseline HBsAg ≤1000 IU/ml, compared with 0% functional cure in the placebo (standard-of-care alone) group.4 Functional cure occurs when the hepatitis B virus DNA and viral protein – HBsAg – are undetectable for at least 24 weeks after stopping all hepatitis B treatment. This indicates the disease is controlled by the immune system without medication. "For many people in Canada living with CHB, the diagnosis comes with years of worry about liver damage and the impact on work, family and future plans, and it can still carry stigma and misunderstanding in their communities," added Jennifer Nebesky, CEO, Liver Canada. "New options like Hibsago can offer people living with CHB greater confidence about their liver health and help us tackle the ongoing challenges that still affect the CHB community across Canada." Story Continues CHB is a long-term viral infection, which affects an estimated 262,000 people in Canada.1 It often goes undetected for years because many people have few or no symptoms, but over time it can lead to serious liver damage, including cirrhosis and liver cancer.2 Hibsago's approval in Canada was granted under Health Canada's Priority Review policy, reflecting its innovation and potential to address a disease with high unmet medical need. It follows authorizations in Japan and the United States. Hibsago supply is anticipated to become available in Canada to eligible patients in early 2027. About chronic hepatitis B Hepatitis B is a viral infection that can cause both acute and chronic liver disease. Chronic hepatitis B occurs when the immune system is unable to clear the virus, resulting in long-lasting infection that affects an estimated 262,000 people in Canada. The disease causes approximately 1.1 million deaths each year globally.1 In people with chronic hepatitis B, a loss in hepatitis B surface antigen is associated with up to an 89% reduction in risk of liver cancer and a 62% reduction in risk of all-cause mortality.5 Many patients require lifelong antiviral therapy for viral suppression, making functional cure a critical goal in disease management. The Canadian Association for the Study of the Liver (CASL) and the Association of Medical Microbiology and Infectious Disease Canada (AMMI) updated guidelines identify functional cure as the optimal goal of treatment.6 About Hibsago (bepirovirsen) Hibsago (bepirovirsen) is an antisense oligonucleotide (ASO) designed to recognise and inhibit the production of the genetic components (i.e. RNA) of the hepatitis B virus that can lead to chronic disease, potentially allowing a person's immune system to regain control. Hibsago reduces the production of RNA and viral proteins associated with HBV, suppresses the level of hepatitis B surface antigen (HBsAg) in the blood, and stimulates the immune system to increase the chances of a durable and sustained response. GSK licensed Hibsago (bepirovirsen) from Ionis Pharmaceuticals and collaborated with them on its development. Please consult the Hibsago product monograph at gsk.ca for complete safety information. The product monograph is also available by calling 1-800-387-7374. About B-Well Clinical trial programs B-Well 1 and B-Well 2 trials are global multi-centre, randomised, double-blind, placebo-controlled trials conducted in 29 countries. They assessed the efficacy, safety, pharmacokinetic profile, and the durability of functional cure in nucleos(t)ide analogue (NA)-treated participants with CHB and baseline surface antigen (HBsAg) ≤3000 IU/ml. The primary endpoint assessed the proportion of participants achieving functional cure in patients with baseline surface antigen (HBsAg) ≤3000 IU/ml. Functional cure in patients with baseline HBsAg ≤1000 IU/ml was a key secondary endpoint. Functional cure was defined as sustained suppression (24 weeks or longer) of HBV DNA ( About GSK GSK is a global biopharma company with a purpose to unite science, technology, and talent to get ahead of disease together. Find out more at www.gsk.ca. Hibsago(贝匹罗韦森)在加拿大获批,成为第一个也是唯一一个可实现 功能性治愈 或 慢性乙型肝炎 的有限疗法 - 美通社PR-Newswire

Hibsago (bepirovirsen) approved in Canada as the first and only finite therapy indicated to achieve functional cure of chronic hepatitis B - PR Newswire Canada
新闻简报2026 年 11 月 3 日,加拿大卫生部批准 Hibsago(bepirovirsen)作为第一个也是唯一一个能够实现 慢性乙型肝炎 的有限疗法。此次批准是根据加拿大卫生部的优先审查政策获得的,强调了该药物的创新性和解决重大未满足医疗需求的潜力。该决定基于 III 期 B-Well 试验的数据,该试验证明了具有临床意义的 功能性治愈 率。具体而言,试验显示,基线 HBsAg ≤3000 IU/ml 的成人中,功能性治愈 的发生率为 19%,HBsAg ≤1000 IU/ml 的成人中,功能性治愈 的发生率为 26%,而安慰剂组为 0%。功能性治愈 被定义为 乙型肝炎病毒 DNA 和病毒蛋白 (HBsAg) 在 治疗 停止后至少 24 周内无法检测到。此次批准反映了解决 慢性乙型肝炎 问题的重要性,该问题影响了加拿大约 262,000 人,并且是肝癌的主要原因。这些试验在 29 个国家进行,是全球多中心、随机、双盲和安慰剂对照的试验。
查看新闻五要素
时间
2026年11月03日,07:00 ET(N7)。
地点与对象
加拿大,估计有 262,000 人患有 慢性乙型肝炎 (N7)。
事件
加拿大卫生部批准 Hibsago(贝匹罗韦森)作为第一个也是唯一一个能够实现 慢性乙型肝炎 (N6) 的有限疗法。
规模、影响与风险
III 期 B-Well 试验证明,基线 HBsAg ≤3000 IU/ml 的成人中 功能性治愈 率为 19%,基线 HBsAg ≤1000 IU/ml 的成人中 功能性治愈 率为 26%,而安慰剂组 (N13) 中的 功能性治愈 率为 0%。
应对、状态与不确定性
该批准基于 III 期 B-Well 试验的数据,由于 慢性乙型肝炎 (N7) 的医疗需求高度未得到满足,因此采用了加拿大卫生部的优先审查政策。
B routine priorityPublic-health news

Hibsago (bepirovirsen) approved in Canada as the first and only finite therapy indicated to achieve functional cure of chronic hepatitis B - PR Newswire Canada

News Brief
On November 3, 2026, Health Canada approved Hibsago (bepirovirsen) as the first and only finite therapy indicated to achieve a functional cure for chronic hepatitis B. This approval was made under Health Canada's Priority Review policy, highlighting the drug's innovation and potential to address a significant unmet medical need. The decision was based on data from Phase III B-Well trials, which demonstrated clinically meaningful functional cure rates. Specifically, the trials showed a 19% functional cure rate in adults with ≤3000 IU/ml baseline HBsAg and a 26% rate in those with ≤1000 IU/ml, compared to 0% in the placebo group. Functional cure is defined as the undetectability of hepatitis B virus DNA and viral protein (HBsAg) for at least 24 weeks after treatment cessation. The approval reflects the importance of addressing chronic hepatitis B, which affects an estimated 262,000 people in Canada and is a leading cause of liver cancer. The trials were conducted in 29 countries and were global multi-centre, randomised, double-blind, and placebo-controlled.

Nov 03, 2026, 07:00 ET Share this article Share this article Hibsago was reviewed under Health Canada's Priority Review policy, reflecting its innovation and potential to address a disease with high unmet medical need Approval is based on data from Phase III B-Well trials which demonstrated significant and clinically meaningful functional cure rates An estimated 262,000 people in Canada live with chronic hepatitis B1, a leading cause of liver cancer2 MISSISSAUGA, ON, Nov. 3, 2026 /CNW/ -- GSK announced today that Health Canada has approved Hibsago (bepirovirsen) as a finite treatment to achieve functional cure of chronic hepatitis B (CHB) virus infection in non-cirrhotic adult patients who have been on nucleos(t)ide analogue therapy for at least 6 months and with HBV DNA <90 IU/mL, and have hepatitis B surface antigen (HBsAg) levels ≤3,000 IU/mL. Clinical efficacy is higher with lower baseline HBsAg levels, especially in patients with baseline HBsAg levels ≤1,000 IU/mL.3 Hibsago is an antisense oligonucleotide (ASO) and the first and only therapy in Canada with an indication to achieve functional cure in CHB. Continue Reading Hibsago logo "Hibsago's authorization in Canada represents an important step forward for people living with CHB, a serious, progressive liver disease," says Dr. Michelle Horn, Country Medical Director, GSK Canada. "The possibility of a functional cure delivered through a finite course of therapy changes expectations from lifelong management to the prospect of stopping treatment and supporting long-term liver health. CHB remains an area of high unmet need, and GSK remains committed to advancing hepatology to help address this gap for Canadians living with this disease." Approval in Canada was supported by results from the B-Well phase III trials showing unprecedented functional cure rates of 19% in adults with ≤3000 IU/ml baseline HBsAg and 26% in adults with baseline HBsAg ≤1000 IU/ml, compared with 0% functional cure in the placebo (standard-of-care alone) group.4 Functional cure occurs when the hepatitis B virus DNA and viral protein – HBsAg – are undetectable for at least 24 weeks after stopping all hepatitis B treatment. This indicates the disease is controlled by the immune system without medication. "For many people in Canada living with CHB, the diagnosis comes with years of worry about liver damage and the impact on work, family and future plans, and it can still carry stigma and misunderstanding in their communities," added Jennifer Nebesky, CEO, Liver Canada. "New options like Hibsago can offer people living with CHB greater confidence about their liver health and help us tackle the ongoing challenges that still affect the CHB community across Canada." CHB is a long-term viral infection, which affects an estimated 262,000 people in Canada.1 It often goes undetected for years because many people have few or no symptoms, but over time it can lead to serious liver damage, including cirrhosis and liver cancer.2 Hibsago's approval in Canada was granted under Health Canada's Priority Review policy, reflecting its innovation and potential to address a disease with high unmet medical need. It follows authorizations in Japan and the United States. Hibsago supply is anticipated to become available in Canada to eligible patients in early 2027. About chronic hepatitis B Hepatitis B is a viral infection that can cause both acute and chronic liver disease. Chronic hepatitis B occurs when the immune system is unable to clear the virus, resulting in long-lasting infection that affects an estimated 262,000 people in Canada. The disease causes approximately 1.1 million deaths each year globally.1 In people with chronic hepatitis B, a loss in hepatitis B surface antigen is associated with up to an 89% reduction in risk of liver cancer and a 62% reduction in risk of all-cause mortality.5 Many patients require lifelong antiviral therapy for viral suppression, making functional cure a critical goal in disease management. The Canadian Association for the Study of the Liver (CASL) and the Association of Medical Microbiology and Infectious Disease Canada (AMMI) updated guidelines identify functional cure as the optimal goal of treatment.6 About Hibsago (bepirovirsen) Hibsago (bepirovirsen) is an antisense oligonucleotide (ASO) designed to recognise and inhibit the production of the genetic components (i.e. RNA) of the hepatitis B virus that can lead to chronic disease, potentially allowing a person's immune system to regain control. Hibsago reduces the production of RNA and viral proteins associated with HBV, suppresses the level of hepatitis B surface antigen (HBsAg) in the blood, and stimulates the immune system to increase the chances of a durable and sustained response. GSK licensed Hibsago (bepirovirsen) from Ionis Pharmaceuticals and collaborated with them on its development. Please consult the Hibsago product monograph at gsk.ca for complete safety information. The product monograph is also available by calling 1-800-387-7374. About B-Well Clinical trial programs B-Well 1 and B-Well 2 trials are global multi-centre, randomised, double-blind, placebo-controlled trials conducted in 29 countries. They assessed the efficacy, safety, pharmacokinetic profile, and the durability of functional cure in nucleos(t)ide analogue (NA)-treated participants with CHB and baseline surface antigen (HBsAg) ≤3000 IU/ml. The primary endpoint assessed the proportion of participants achieving functional cure in patients with baseline surface antigen (HBsAg) ≤3000 IU/ml. Functional cure in patients with baseline HBsAg ≤1000 IU/ml was a key secondary endpoint. Functional cure was defined as sustained suppression (24 weeks or longer) of HBV DNA (<LLOQ) and HBsAg not detected, with or without hepatitis B surface antibodies, after a finite duration of therapy with bepirovirsen and off all HBV treatment. About GSK GSK is a global biopharma company with a purpose to unite science, technology, and talent to get ahead of disease together. Find out more at www.gsk.ca. B 常规重要公共卫生新闻

美国 FDA 批准贝匹罗韦森用于 慢性乙型肝炎 治疗 — ScienceAlert - UA.NEWS

US FDA approves bepirovirsen for chronic hepatitis B treatment — ScienceAlert - UA.NEWS
新闻简报The US Food and Drug Administration (FDA) has approved bepirovirsen, marketed as Hibsago, for the treatment of chronic hepatitis B, as reported by ScienceAlert on 05 November 2026. This decision followed the results of two Phase 3 clinical trials published in the New England Journal of Medicine, which showed that a six-month course of bepirovirsen achieved a functional cure in 19% of participants (233 out of 1,220), compared to 0% in the placebo group. The drug suppresses the virus to undetectable levels in some patients, but the long-term effect remains uncertain. The FDA emphasized the need for regular blood tests during treatment, and some participants experienced serious adverse reactions, including liver inflammation. GSK and researchers plan to explore combinations of bepirovirsen with other drugs to enhance its effectiveness. This approval marks a significant development in the management of chronic hepatitis B, offering a potential new therapeutic option.
查看新闻五要素
时间
The US FDA approved bepirovirsen for chronic hepatitis B treatment on 05 November 2026, as reported in the article published on the same date.
地点与对象
该批准与美国相关,针对的是 慢性乙型肝炎 患者,这是一种由 乙型肝炎病毒 引起的人类感染。
事件
美国食品和药物管理局 (FDA) 基于《新英格兰医学杂志》上发表的两项 3 期临床试验结果,批准贝匹罗韦森用于 慢性乙型肝炎 的 治疗。
规模、影响与风险
为期 6 个月的贝匹罗韦森疗程为 19% 的参与者(1,220 人中的 233 人)提供了 功能性治愈,而安慰剂组(614 人中的 0 人)没有记录到这样的结果。该药物可能会将某些患者的病毒抑制到检测不到的水平,但长期效果仍有待确定。
应对、状态与不确定性
FDA 已批准该药物,但警告在 治疗 期间需要定期进行血液检查。一些试验参与者出现了严重的不良反应,包括肝脏炎症。该公司计划研究贝匹罗韦森与其他药物的组合以提高疗效。
B routine priorityPublic-health news

US FDA approves bepirovirsen for chronic hepatitis B treatment — ScienceAlert - UA.NEWS

News Brief
The US Food and Drug Administration (FDA) has approved bepirovirsen, marketed as Hibsago, for the treatment of chronic hepatitis B, as reported by ScienceAlert on 05 November 2026. This decision followed the results of two Phase 3 clinical trials published in the New England Journal of Medicine, which showed that a six-month course of bepirovirsen achieved a functional cure in 19% of participants (233 out of 1,220), compared to 0% in the placebo group. The drug suppresses the virus to undetectable levels in some patients, but the long-term effect remains uncertain. The FDA emphasized the need for regular blood tests during treatment, and some participants experienced serious adverse reactions, including liver inflammation. GSK and researchers plan to explore combinations of bepirovirsen with other drugs to enhance its effectiveness. This approval marks a significant development in the management of chronic hepatitis B, offering a potential new therapeutic option.
Original source text (English)
News Pharmaceuticals US FDA approves bepirovirsen for chronic hepatitis B treatment — ScienceAlert UA.NEWS 05 November 2026 05:07 The US Food and Drug Administration (FDA) has approved bepirovirsen, marketed under the brand name Hibsago, for the treatment of chronic hepatitis B. The decision was made after two Phase 3 clinical trials, the results of which were published in the New England Journal of Medicine, ScienceAlert reports . The drug was developed by the pharmaceutical company GSK. The company calls bepirovirsen the first treatment that may provide a so-called functional cure for chronic hepatitis B: the virus in the blood is suppressed to an undetectable level, and in some patients infection control persists after therapy is discontinued. At the same time, traces of the virus remain in the body, and the duration of this effect over many years has yet to be established. Study results A six-month course of bepirovirsen provided a functional cure in 233 of 1,220 participants, or 19%. No such outcome was recorded in the placebo group: 0 of 614 participants. In patients who responded to treatment, the virus remained undetectable for at least 24 weeks after all therapy was stopped — that is, 72 weeks after the start of the study. More current news is available on the UA.News Telegram channel Telegram . Bepirovirsen is administered through weekly injections alongside existing oral antiviral drugs. For patients who achieved the required outcome, these medications could also be discontinued. The likelihood of an effect was higher in people with lower initial levels of a key protein associated with chronic hepatitis B. Adverse reactions and monitoring The FDA warned of the need for regular blood tests during treatment. Some trial participants experienced serious adverse reactions, including liver inflammation. GSK and researchers also plan to study combinations of bepirovirsen with other drugs to increase the effectiveness of therapy. Read us on Download our app
View five news elements
Time
The US FDA approved bepirovirsen for chronic hepatitis B treatment on 05 November 2026, as reported in the article published on the same date.
Location and population
The approval is relevant to the United States, targeting patients with chronic hepatitis B, a human infection caused by the hepatitis B virus.
Event
The US Food and Drug Administration (FDA) approved bepirovirsen for the treatment of chronic hepatitis B, based on results from two Phase 3 clinical trials published in the New England Journal of Medicine.
Scale, impact and risk
A six-month course of bepirovirsen provided a functional cure in 19% of participants (233 out of 1,220), while no such outcome was recorded in the placebo group (0 out of 614). The drug may suppress the virus to undetectable levels in some patients, but the long-term effect remains to be established.
Response, status and uncertainty
The FDA has approved the drug, but it warned of the need for regular blood tests during treatment. Some trial participants experienced serious adverse reactions, including liver inflammation. The company plans to study combinations of bepirovirsen with other drugs to increase effectiveness.

NHC 支持仪表板警报,以帮助找到“无声杀手”乙型肝炎 - 新西兰医生

NHC backs dashboard alerts to help find the ‘silent killer’ hepatitis B - New Zealand Doctor
新闻简报New Zealand Doctor 于 2026 年 11 月 4 日发表的文章报道称,国家 Hauora 联盟 (NHC) 和新西兰肝炎基金会正在推广仪表板警报,以鼓励乙型肝炎检测。该计划针对可能未接受过乙型肝炎 疫苗 的 40 岁以上人群,旨在识别未确诊病例并为患者提供适当的护理。文章没有提供具体的病例数、死亡数或暴露数,但证实了这一检测举措的推广。响应状态表明该举措正处于实施阶段,证据中没有提供有关测试或接触者追踪的进一步细节。
查看新闻五要素
时间
该文章于2026-11-04发表。事件日期在证据中未明确说明。
地点与对象
这篇文章与新西兰有关,专门针对卫生专业人员和可能未感染过乙型肝炎的 40 岁以上普通人群 疫苗。
事件
国家 Hauora 联盟 (NHC) 和新西兰肝炎基金会正在推广仪表板警报以提示乙型肝炎检测。这是艾伦·佩罗特 (Alan Perrott) 报道的一项已证实的举措。
规模、影响与风险
该倡议旨在发现未确诊的乙型肝炎病例,并为患者提供护理服务,但证据中没有报告具体的病例数、死亡人数或暴露人数。
应对、状态与不确定性
目前,该倡议正在积极推广并处于实施阶段。证据中没有提及调查、测试、隔离或接触者追踪状态。
B routine priorityPublic-health news

NHC backs dashboard alerts to help find the ‘silent killer’ hepatitis B - New Zealand Doctor

News Brief
The article, published on November 4, 2026, by New Zealand Doctor, reports that the National Hauora Coalition (NHC) and the Hepatitis Foundation of New Zealand are promoting dashboard alerts to encourage hepatitis B testing. The initiative targets individuals aged 40-plus who may not have received the hepatitis B vaccine, aiming to identify undiagnosed cases and connect patients with appropriate care. The article does not provide specific case counts, death counts, or exposure numbers, but it confirms the promotion of this testing initiative. The response status indicates that the initiative is in the implementation phase, with no further details on testing or contact tracing provided in the evidence.
Original source text (English)

B 常规重要公共卫生新闻

IDSA:超过一半的 5 岁以下儿童通过家庭接触接触乙型肝炎

IDSA: More Than Half of Children Under 5 Exposed to Hep B Exposed Via Household Contact
新闻简报2023 年美国传染病学会 (IDWeek) 会议上提出并在 2026 年 11 月 5 日的新闻稿中报道的一项研究发现,超过一半的 5 岁以下儿童接触过 乙型肝炎病毒 (HBV),是通过家庭接触而不是其生母接触的。这项研究由加利福尼亚州普莱森顿的 Kaiser Permanente 研究部进行,分析了 131,800 个家庭中 158,169 名 5 岁以下儿童的数据,其中至少有一名 KPNC 成员。其中,2,024 名儿童 (1.3%) 生活在患有活动性乙型肝炎病毒感染的家庭中。研究显示,其中 58.2% 的儿童是通过家庭接触接触的,这凸显了 HBV 传播 对婴儿预防已知和未知来源的 传播 的重要性。研究结果强调,除了孕产妇 传播 之外,还需要更广泛的认识和预防措施。
查看新闻五要素
时间
该研究在2023年10月21日至24日举行的美国传染病学会(IDWeek)年会上提出。数据收集于2023年6月16日,研究结果于2026年11月5日报告。
地点与对象
该研究的重点是居住在至少还有一名北加州凯撒医疗机构 (KPNC) 成员的家庭中的 5 岁以下儿童。这些家庭位于美国,特别是 KPNC 系统中。
事件
加利福尼亚州普莱森顿 Kaiser Permanente 研究部的研究人员进行的一项研究发现,超过一半的 5 岁以下儿童接触 乙型肝炎病毒 (HBV) 是通过家庭接触而不是其生母接触的。HealthDay News 报道了这一发现,并在 IDWeek 会议上进行了介绍。
规模、影响与风险
在 2,024 名接触 HBV 的儿童中,58.2% 是家庭接触者。该研究确定了 1,689 个活跃的 HBV 感染家庭,其中 1.3% 的 5 岁以下儿童居住在此类家庭中。人们注意到,家庭接触者(不仅限于生母)感染 HBV 传播 的风险很大。
应对、状态与不确定性
该研究是一项回顾性 队列研究 分析,研究结果基于从 KPNC 成员收集的数据。研究人员强调了 HBV 传播 对婴儿预防已知和未知来源的 传播 的重要性。该研究的结果在一份新闻稿中报告,但没有提供有关调查状态的进一步细节。
B routine priorityPublic-health news

IDSA: More Than Half of Children Under 5 Exposed to Hep B Exposed Via Household Contact

News Brief
A study presented at the Infectious Diseases Society of America (IDWeek) conference in 2023, and reported in a press release on November 5, 2026, found that more than half of children under 5 years exposed to hepatitis B virus (HBV) were exposed via household contact other than their birth mother. The research, conducted by the Kaiser Permanente Division of Research in Pleasanton, California, analyzed data from 158,169 children under 5 living in 131,800 households with at least one additional KPNC member. Of these, 2,024 children (1.3%) lived in households with active HBV infections. The study revealed that 58.2% of these children were exposed through household contacts, highlighting the importance of HBV vaccination for infants to prevent transmission from both known and unknown sources. The findings underscore the need for broader awareness and preventive measures beyond maternal transmission.
Original source text (English)
Menu SECTIONS News Features CME Conferences Clinical Tools Special Collections Multimedia Fact Sheets Diagnosis & Disease Info Topics Acute Coronary Syndrome Arrhythmia Coronary Artery Disease General Cardiology General Medicine Heart Failure Hypertension Interventional Metabolic Myocardial and Pericardial Disease Pediatric Cardiology Practice Management Transplantation Valvular Heart Disease Vascular Disease Haymarket Medical Network Cancer Therapy Advisor Cardiology Advisor Clinical Advisor Clinical Pain Advisor Dermatology Advisor Endocrinology Advisor Gastroenterology Advisor Hematology Advisor Infectious Disease Advisor Medical Bag MPR Neurology Advisor Obesity Advisor Oncology Nurse Advisor Ophthalmology Advisor Optometry Advisor Psychiatry Advisor Pulmonology Advisor Rare Disease Advisor Renal and Urology News Rheumatology Advisor Sleep Wake Advisor Vaccine Advisor Search for: News CME Drugs Charts Therapies Calculators Resources Meetings Part of the News and Features CME Conferences Clinical Tools Special Collections Multimedia Share this article Share on Facebook Share on Twitter Share on LinkedIn Share on Bluesky Share by Email Print Pediatric Cardiology IDSA: More Than Half of Children Under 5 Exposed to Hep B Exposed Via Household Contact Publish Date November 5, 2026 Of the 2024 children exposed to hepatitis B, 41.8% were birth-exposed and 58.2% were household exposed. HealthDay News — More than half of children younger than 5 years exposed to hepatitis B virus (HBV) are exposed by someone in their household other than their birth mother, according to a study presented at the annual meeting of the Infectious Diseases Society of America (IDWeek), held from Oct. 21 to 24 in Washington, D.C. Karen Jacobson, M.D., M.P.H., from the Kaiser Permanente Division of Research in Pleasanton, California, and colleagues examined the HBV status of household contacts of young children in Kaiser Permanente Northern California (KPNC) in a retrospective cohort study including KPNC members aged younger than 5 years on June 16, 2023. A total of 158,169 children younger than 5 years living in 131,800 households with at least one additional KPNC member were identified. Of the households, 1,689 included a person with active HBV; 2,024 children (1.3 percent) lived in a household with a person with active HBV. The researchers found that 41.8 and 58.2 percent of the 2,024 exposed children were birth-exposed and household-exposed, respectively. Compared with HBV-positive mothers, nonmaternal HBV-positive household contacts were older (44 versus 38 years); 76.5 percent were male. All birth-exposed children received HBV vaccination within seven days of birth compared with 93.5 and 86.7 percent of household-exposed and unexposed children, respectively. “A child can be exposed to hepatitis B from anyone with an active infection, not just their mother,” Jacobson said in a statement. “No one knows the status of everyone a child may come into contact with, and vaccinating infants at birth protects against known and unknown potential sources of transmission. The hepatitis B vaccine is a safe and effective way to protect against HBV infection.” Press Release More Information
View five news elements
Time
The study was presented at the annual meeting of the Infectious Diseases Society of America (IDWeek) held from October 21 to 24, 2023. The data was collected on June 16, 2023, and the findings were reported on November 5, 2026.
Location and population
The study focused on children younger than 5 years living in households with at least one additional Kaiser Permanente Northern California (KPNC) member. The households were located in the United States, specifically in the KPNC system.
Event
A study conducted by researchers at the Kaiser Permanente Division of Research in Pleasanton, California, found that more than half of children under 5 years exposed to hepatitis B virus (HBV) were exposed via household contact other than their birth mother. This finding was reported by HealthDay News and presented at the IDWeek conference.
Scale, impact and risk
Out of 2,024 children exposed to HBV, 58.2% were household-exposed. The study identified 1,689 households with active HBV infections, and 1.3% of children under 5 lived in such households. The risk of HBV transmission was noted to be significant through household contacts, not limited to birth mothers.
Response, status and uncertainty
The study was a retrospective cohort analysis, and the findings were based on data collected from KPNC members. The researchers emphasized the importance of HBV vaccination for infants to prevent transmission from both known and unknown sources. The study's results were reported in a press release, and no further details on the investigation status were provided.

乙肝治疗迎来新进展:什么是医学上期待的“功能性治愈”? |#科学资讯# #医学# #慢性乙型肝炎#

Original source text (Chinese)
新闻 体育 汽车 房产 旅游 教育 时尚 科技 财经 娱乐 乙肝治疗迎来新进展:什么是医学上期待的“功能性治愈”? |#科学资讯# #医学# #慢性乙型肝炎# 2026-11-05 12:05 来源: 常识技能百宝箱 对于数以百万计的慢性乙型肝炎(Chronic Hepatitis B,简称CHB)患者来说,长年累月地服用抗病毒药物是控制病情的常态。这种治疗方式虽然能抑制病毒复制,但往往难以彻底清除病毒,患者一旦停药,病情就有可能反复。近日,美国食品药品监督管理局(FDA)批准了一款名为贝皮罗韦森(Bepirovirsen)的新药,为这一领域带来了一种新的治疗思路。 什么是“功能性治愈”? 在医学语境下,所谓“功能性治愈”,并不是指彻底从体内消灭所有病毒痕迹,而是指通过药物治疗,将血液中的乙肝病毒抑制到检测不到的水平。更关键的是,在停止治疗后,患者的身体依然能够维持这种受控状态,不再需要持续服药。与现有的长期维持治疗相比,这无疑是一个重要的治疗目标。 新药是如何发挥作用的? 这项结论源于发表在《新英格兰医学杂志》(New England Journal of Medicine)上的两项三期临床试验。研究人员对1220名参与者进行了为期六个月的疗程观察。结果显示,约19%的受试者在接受该药物治疗后,实现了功能性治愈,且在停止治疗后的24周内,血液中仍检测不到病毒。相比之下,使用安慰剂的对照组中,没有一人达到这一指标。 科学发现背后的局限性 尽管数据令人振奋,但我们必须保持冷静的科学态度。首先,并非所有患者都能获得同样的治疗效果。研究发现,在治疗开始时,体内乙肝病毒相关蛋白水平较低的患者,实现功能性治愈的概率更高。这意味着该药物可能更适合特定类型的患者,临床应用前需要更精准的筛选。 其次,该药物并非没有风险。临床试验中,部分患者出现了包括肝脏炎症在内的严重副作用,因此在治疗过程中,必须配合定期的血液监测。此外,目前的观察期有限,我们尚不清楚这种“功能性治愈”能否维持数年之久,病毒是否会在更长的时间跨度后卷土重来,这些都需要后续的长期研究来验证。 未来治疗的展望 目前,研究人员正在探索将贝皮罗韦森与其他药物联合使用的可能性,以期进一步提高疗效。对于慢性乙型肝炎这一全球性的公共卫生挑战,任何能够减少患者用药负担、提升生活质量的进展都值得关注。但现阶段,这仅仅是一个良好的开端,而非一劳永逸的终点。 我是胡博士,胡说八道的胡,但胡博士科普向来严谨。欢迎关注,欢迎捉虫。 返回搜狐,查看更多 阅读 () 作者声明 含AI生成内容 平台声明 该文观点仅代表作者本人,搜狐号系信息发布平台,搜狐仅提供信息存储空间服务。
